## 5. External Research Findings

**All findings in this section are external context** from official documentation, package tarballs and project trackers, fetched on 2026-11-07 and 2026-11-08. They are not code observations of this repository. Where an external statement and the codebase disagree, the codebase wins (research rule 6). Provenance for both web files: `provider=WebSearch/WebFetch reason=tavily-unavailable` (no Tavily tool was available to the agents). The WebFetch tool returns a summary made by a small model, so quoted strings are as returned by it; web-02 cross-checked the important GitHub items against raw API output and the npm tarballs.

Relationship values below mean: supports = agrees with a codebase finding; extends = adds a fact the codebase files left UNVERIFIED; contradicts = disagrees with a codebase finding.

### 5.1 Claude Code user-level skills, hooks and live reload

Source group: official Claude Code documentation at `code.claude.com/docs/en/`. Claude Code behaviour in the docs is gated by versions (up to v2.1.314 are mentioned). The Dockerfile pins `@anthropic-ai/claude-code@2.1.289` (`templates/VSCode-AIDev02/image/Dockerfile.coder:110`) [CODE-VERIFIED by reading; the built image was not inspected]. `claude --version` printed `2.1.319` in the web-01 agent's workspace (an observation about that workspace; whether the running image differs from the pin, for example by auto-update, is UNVERIFIED). What stays open is whether the doc-described behaviour holds at the pinned version, not which version is pinned. Every statement below holds for the docs as fetched on 2026-11-08.

**Skill discovery, ranking and live pickup**

- **Finding W01-1:** a personal skill at `~/.claude/skills/<skill-name>/SKILL.md` is available to "all your projects on this machine"; a project skill at `.claude/skills/<skill-name>/SKILL.md` to sessions in that repository; enterprise skills come from the managed settings directory; plugin skills are namespaced.
  - Source URL: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills ("Choose where skills load")
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (answers GL G-02 / UV-07; a user-level stub would appear in every project, including projects that have no BMAD)
- **Finding W01-2:** on a name collision "Enterprise over personal, and personal over project", so `/deploy` runs the personal copy when both exist; a skill beats a same-named `.claude/commands/` file. A user-level stub named like a BMAD skill (for example `bmad-help`) would shadow the project copy that `bmad install` writes. The shadowing is W01's inference from the quoted rule, not tested.
  - Source URL: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills ("Resolve skills that share a name")
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (R03 A3 B step 5 and R01 U7 left the ranking UNVERIFIED; the installer itself would not object because the ancestor check is off, R01 s6)
- **Finding W01-3:** Claude Code watches skill directories and picks up an added, edited or removed skill under `~/.claude/skills/`, the project `.claude/skills/` or an `--add-dir` directory within the current session. Exception: "If you create a top-level skills directory that didn't exist when the session started, run `/reload-skills` to pick up the skills you put there"; that directory is not watched until then. Live detection covers `SKILL.md` text only. In a project that never had BMAD, `.claude/skills/` usually does not exist, so first use inside an open session needs `/reload-skills` or a restart.
  - Source URL: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills ("Edit a skill during a session")
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (answers GL G-01 / Q-1 with a split answer; R03 gap 9 recorded "skills docs not fetched")
- **Finding W01-4:** project skills load from `.claude/skills/` in the directory where Claude Code starts and in every parent up to the repository root; a `.claude/skills/` below the start directory does not load at startup and loads when a file there is read or edited, or after `/add-dir` (v2.1.257 or later).
  - Source URL: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills ("Load skills in monorepos and subdirectories")
  - Relevance: MEDIUM
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (bears on GL D-3: an install at the repository root is found from a subfolder session; an install into a subfolder is not found from the root until a file there is read)
- **Finding W01-5:** custom commands have merged into skills: a `.claude/commands/deploy.md` file and a `.claude/skills/deploy/SKILL.md` both create `/deploy`; existing command files keep working. A user-level command is therefore no cheaper than a user-level skill for a bootstrap stub. An explicit `~/.claude/commands/` row was not in the skills page text; the claude-directory page lists `commands/*.md` under `~/.claude/` and `.claude/` as "Project and global" (URL below).
  - Source URL: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills (opening note); https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-directory
  - Relevance: MEDIUM
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (option B: a command and a skill are discovered the same way)

**Running a command from a skill**

- **Finding W01-6:** a skill can bundle scripts (referenced with `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}`) and can pre-approve tools with `allowed-tools: Bash(<pattern>)` for the turn that invokes it. The `` !`<command>` `` form runs a shell command before the skill text reaches Claude. Injected commands "never prompt for permission while the skill renders" and abort the invocation unless an allow rule or `allowed-tools` matches (outside auto mode); deny and ask rules still win; `disableSkillShellExecution: true` switches the feature off. A Bash call made by Claude after reading the skill prompts by default except for a built-in read-only set.
  - Source URL: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills ("Inject dynamic context", "Pre-approve tools for a skill"); https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions ("Permission system")
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (R03 A3 shared facts expected a prompt and marked it UNVERIFIED; the install command through a stub prompts unless pre-approved, which is a visible consent step). Residual UNVERIFIED: the exact `Bash(...)` pattern grammar for the install command; whether the image sets `disableSkillShellExecution`
- **Finding W01-7:** the docs do not forbid a skill or hook from modifying the project directory and describe skills whose work writes files ("The skill's edits to other files stay, so use git to revert them"; a skill that writes `.claude/skills/verify/SKILL.md` at the repo root).
  - Source URL: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills ("Run and verify your app", "Run skills in a subagent")
  - Relevance: MEDIUM
  - Relationship to codebase: supports (nothing contradicts the finding that the BMAD install writes only inside the project, R01 s5)

**Hooks: inputs, outputs, timing, reload**

- **Finding W01-8:** a command hook receives JSON on stdin with `session_id`, `transcript_path`, `cwd`, `permission_mode`, `hook_event_name`; SessionStart adds `source` (`startup`, `resume`, `clear`, `compact`, `fork`); UserPromptSubmit adds `prompt`; `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` ("the project root where the session started") is exported to the hook process. `cwd` and `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` can differ; how they differ in a Coder workspace is UNVERIFIED.
  - Source URL: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks ("Common input fields", "SessionStart", "UserPromptSubmit", "Reference scripts by path")
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (GL G-08 and D-3: the hook should read `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` for "the project" and treat `cwd` as a hint)
- **Finding W01-9:** SessionStart and UserPromptSubmit add plain-text stdout (or `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext`) to Claude's context; Claude never sees stderr from a hook that exits 0. SessionStart has "No blocking or decision control"; UserPromptSubmit and PreToolUse can block. Whether SessionStart stdout is also shown to the user is not stated: UNVERIFIED.
  - Source URL: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks ("Exit code 0", "SessionStart", "UserPromptSubmit", "PreToolUse")
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (R03 A3 D step 2 and step 3 left the display of hook output UNVERIFIED; a hook can tell Claude that the project lacks BMAD without blocking anything)
- **Finding W01-10:** command-hook `timeout` defaults are 600 s, lowered to 30 s on UserPromptSubmit; a SessionStart hook runs in the background in an interactive session, so typing is possible, but Claude's first response waits for the hooks to finish.
  - Source URL: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks ("Common fields", "SessionStart decision control")
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (R03 A3 D step 2 noted all 29 timeouts in this workspace's `settings.json` are 1 to 10 s; the 30 s UserPromptSubmit default is the tighter bound for a first install whose real duration is UNVERIFIED, GL G-09)
- **Finding W01-11:** a SessionStart hook can return `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"SessionStart","reloadSkills":true}}`, which makes Claude Code re-scan skill and command directories after the SessionStart hooks complete; the docs say files a hook writes into `~/.claude/skills/` or `.claude/skills/` can otherwise be missing at the first prompt. This is the only documented way to make freshly written project skills visible in the same session without the user typing `/reload-skills`. No equivalent field was found for UserPromptSubmit.
  - Source URL: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks ("SessionStart decision control", "Reload skills that a hook installs")
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (changes how option D should be shaped: SessionStart plus `reloadSkills`, not UserPromptSubmit)
- **Finding W01-12:** `reloadSkills` was introduced in Claude Code v2.1.152 according to a user-authored issue that quotes the changelog; the number could not be reproduced in first-party text.
  - Source URL: https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/issues/62664
  - Relevance: MEDIUM
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (the image pins Claude Code 2.1.289, `Dockerfile.coder:110`, above the 2.1.152 quoted here; whether the field works at the pin is UNVERIFIED; secondary source, lower reliability)
- **Finding W01-13:** edits to `hooks` in settings files are picked up by the file watcher in a running session; a settings file created mid-session loads if its folder existed at start, and for the project `.claude/` folder even if the folder is created in that session. This differs from skills (W01-3).
  - Source URL: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings ("When edits take effect"); https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks ("Disable or remove hooks")
  - Relevance: MEDIUM
  - Relationship to codebase: supports (R03 s6 recorded this doc-sourced and UNVERIFIED, UV-42; closed from official text, not tested live)
- **Finding W01-14:** "Command hooks execute shell commands with your full user permissions. They can modify, delete, or access any files your user account can access." Hooks run by Claude Code itself and not through the Bash tool; that a command hook therefore runs without a Bash permission prompt is the best reading, but no sentence says it.
  - Source URL: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks ("Security considerations")
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (GL G-04: a hook removes the user's chance to say no, R03 A3 D step 2)

**Hook scopes, managed settings, trust**

- **Finding W01-15:** hooks in `~/.claude/settings.json` apply to "all your projects"; user, project, local and managed hook entries merge rather than replace; an identical handler defined twice runs once; matching hooks run in parallel.
  - Source URL: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks ("Hook locations", "Hook handler fields"); https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings ("Settings files and who they affect")
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: supports (R03 s5 found that `superclaude install` merges hooks additively; a user-level hook fires in every project, including repositories where BMAD must not be installed, so the hook must carry the "is this a project that wants BMAD, and does it lack `_bmad/`" test)
- **Finding W01-16:** `/etc/claude-code/managed-settings.d/*.json` is a documented location, merged after `managed-settings.json` in alphabetical order (single values replace, lists combine without duplicates, nested blocks merge by key); `hooks` is a list-merged key and managed policy is a documented hook location. No single sentence says a drop-in may hold `hooks`: documented by inference. Managed hooks cannot be disabled by the user's `disableAllHooks`.
  - Source URL: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/managed-settings ("Split a file-based policy across teams", "How Claude Code combines managed sources"); https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks ("Hook locations")
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (answers GL G-03 / UV-25 half for option D2; the existing drop-in `10-retention.json` sets only `cleanupPeriodDays`, R03 s4)
- **Finding W01-17:** two managed keys would remove user-level customisation: `allowManagedHooksOnly` restricts which hooks run, and `strictPluginOnlyCustomization` blocks skills, agents, hooks and MCP servers from user and project sources. If a file under `/etc/claude-code/` in the Coder image set either key, options B and D would stop working. Remote managed settings are fetched only when the session authenticates to Anthropic's API directly or through a gateway; whether a Coder workspace sets `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` was not established.
  - Source URL: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/managed-settings ("How Claude Code combines managed sources")
  - Relevance: MEDIUM
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (the image's `/etc/claude-code/` content is a codebase check, not an external one; only `10-retention.json` is known)
- **Finding W01-18:** in an interactive session Claude Code holds back hooks from every settings file, including `~/.claude/settings.json`, until the user accepts the workspace trust dialog for the folder or a parent whose trust extends to it; `-p` and SDK sessions treat the folder as trusted. A user-level SessionStart hook therefore does not run in a never-trusted folder until trust is accepted. Whether a held-back hook runs later in the same session is not stated: UNVERIFIED. Skills are not gated the same way ("Workspace trust never gates a skill's `allowed-tools` in any session").
  - Source URL: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks ("Security considerations", "Workspace trust"); https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions ("What runs before you trust a folder")
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (a new constraint on option D that no codebase file considered; it applies to project-level hooks too, so no hook-based option escapes it)

**Other**

- **Finding W01-19:** every skill in the listing adds to context on every turn, so a user-level stub adds one listing entry to every project.
  - Source URL: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills
  - Relevance: LOW
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (cost of a user-level artifact, GL G-02 blast radius)

Not answered by official sources (UNVERIFIED): timing of a held-back hook after trust is accepted; whether SessionStart stdout is shown to the user; the meaning of `skipDangerousModePermissionPrompt`; whether the Orca `PermissionRequest` hook decides permissions (not a Claude Code docs question); the Bash permission pattern grammar for the install command; first-party text for the `reloadSkills` version. Discrepancies with the codebase findings: none CODE-CONTRADICTED (W01 "Discrepancies" section); the log's Q-1 ("the settings page does not say") is answered by the skills page.

### 5.2 Upstream BMAD-METHOD user-scope and new-project install

Source group: the npm registry, the published `bmad-method` tarballs, the GitHub REST API and raw files of `bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD`, and the project's documentation. The web-02 agent used read-only `curl` GET requests; it ran no `npm`, `npx` or installer, and extracted tarballs only into `/tmp/web02-*` directories outside any repository. Only the 6.9.0, 6.10.0 and 6.12.4 tarballs were searched. Not read: community posts, blog posts and forum threads (low reliability), the `-next` prereleases, the `V6.12` branch source.

**Versions and the user-scope question**

- **Finding W02-1:** `bmad-method` `latest` is 6.12.4 (published 2026-10-14); 6.9.0 (this repo's pin) was published 2026-06-22; stable lines after it are 6.10.0 (2026-07-03), 6.11.0 (2026-08-10), 6.12.0 (2026-09-04), 6.12.1 (2026-10-04), 6.12.2 (2026-10-11), 6.12.3 and 6.12.4 (both 2026-10-14). The "Update Available 6.12.4" banner seen in the CLI is therefore genuine. All of these require Node `>=20.12.0`, the same as 6.9.0.
  - Source URL: https://registry.npmjs.org/bmad-method
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (answers the existence half of GL G-05 / UV-20, which R02 U2 and R01 U9 left UNVERIFIED)
- **Finding W02-2:** the token `global_target_dir` appears in exactly one file, `tools/installer/ide/platform-codes.yaml`, with 43 hits in 6.9.0, 43 in 6.10.0 and 46 in 6.12.4; no `.js` file in any of the three packages reads it. The 6.12.4 header still describes it as "User-home directory for global install". No published release up to 6.12.4 implements a user-scope or global skill install. A newer pin of `bmad-method` therefore does not make option F available.
  - Source URL: https://registry.npmjs.org/bmad-method/-/bmad-method-6.9.0.tgz, https://registry.npmjs.org/bmad-method/-/bmad-method-6.10.0.tgz, https://registry.npmjs.org/bmad-method/-/bmad-method-6.12.4.tgz (Grep of the extracted packages)
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: supports (R01 s2: 43 hits, one file, zero consumers in 6.9.0), and extends it to 6.10.0 and 6.12.4. Not searched: the 6.11.0 and 6.12.0 to 6.12.3 tarballs individually; 6.12.4 is the newest package and shows no consumer
- **Finding W02-3:** the `install` command options in 6.12.4 (`commands/install.js:12-53`) differ from 6.9.0 (`:12-51`) only by `--shims` and `--no-shims`; neither version has a `--global`, `--user`, `--scope` or `--home` flag. The word "scope" in the installer code means team-scope versus user-scope CONFIG keys (`_bmad/config.toml` against `_bmad/config.user.toml`), not an install location. Other `os.homedir()` uses in 6.12.4 are module caches under `~/.bmad/cache/` and `~` expansion of typed paths.
  - Source URL: https://registry.npmjs.org/bmad-method/-/bmad-method-6.12.4.tgz (`tools/installer/commands/install.js`, `set-overrides.js`, `external-manager.js`)
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: supports (R01 s1 and s4: no scope flag; HOME touched only for caches). Trap for later research: release notes or issue titles that mention "user scope" mean config keys
- **Finding W02-4:** `global_target_dir` was added to every platform as data "for parity with the Vercel skills ecosystem" (issue #2313: "`global_target_dir` added to every platform (XDG for Crush; tool-specific for Codex; shared `~/.agents/skills/` for 11 platforms)"), never wired to an install option in 6.x.
  - Source URL: https://github.com/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD/issues/2313
  - Relevance: MEDIUM
  - Relationship to codebase: supports (R01 SD-01: the field is inert data; the origin confirms it)

**Release notes and installer behaviour after 6.9.0**

- **Finding W02-5:** none of the eight release notes from 6.9.0 to 6.12.4 announces a global or user-level install; the only user-home skill paths named (CodeWhale, Antigravity CLI) are platform-table data in the same inert field.
  - Source URL: https://api.github.com/repos/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD/releases?per_page=30; https://github.com/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD/releases/tag/v6.12.4 (and the same pattern for the other tags)
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: supports (R01 s2, s5)
- **Finding W02-6:** install-affecting changes after 6.9.0, quoted from the release bodies: 6.12.2 "`install --yes` without `--directory` installs into the current directory instead of stopping at a prompt (#3064)"; 6.11.0 "`--set core.<key>` overrides were applied too late (#2671)... artifact paths and the created directory still used `_bmad-output/`"; 6.11.0 core-only installs; 6.12.0 "Deprecated shims are opt-in on fresh installs. Pass `--shims`"; 6.12.1 user-scope key defaults now live in `config.toml` so skills render on a fresh clone without `config.user.toml` (#3016); 6.9.0 and 6.11.0 "a missing `uv` warns... never blocks" (from 6.11.0 the Build variants halt without `uv`). The 6.12.2 wording implies that 6.9.0 without `--directory` stopped at a prompt; our command always passes `--directory`. That 6.9.0 behaviour is UNVERIFIED (not run).
  - Source URL: https://api.github.com/repos/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD/releases?per_page=30
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: supports (R01 s3.2: `--directory` avoids the directory prompt; `--output-folder _bmad-output` stays in every command; `uv` is advisory in 6.9.0) and extends (explains `--set` ordering for GL G-07 and G-14). A pin bump would need its own plan, Renovate handling and a CI run (GL D-7)
- **Finding W02-7:** the 6.9.0 skills run their config resolver as `python3 {project-root}/_bmad/scripts/...` (41 lines in 31 `SKILL.md`, re-observed on the tarball); 6.12.4 skills use `uv run` (105 lines in 39 files, 0 `python3 `). The 6.12.4 `bmm-skills` layout differs (`agents/`, `plan/`, `ship/`, `v6-shims/` instead of `1-analysis/` to `4-implementation/`).
  - Source URL: https://registry.npmjs.org/bmad-method/-/bmad-method-6.9.0.tgz and https://registry.npmjs.org/bmad-method/-/bmad-method-6.12.4.tgz (Grep count of `SKILL.md`)
  - Relevance: MEDIUM
  - Relationship to codebase: supports (R03 A1: 41 lines in 31 files) and extends (GL G-06: a bump would remove the interaction with the UV-only hook at the price of a changed skill set and tree layout; a trade-off for the owner, not a first-use mechanism)
- **Finding W02-8:** 6.12.4 skill text tolerates a failed resolver call ("Missing keys -> neutral defaults; never block" in `bmad-prd` and `bmad-ux`; "On failure or missing values -> neutral defaults; never block" in `bmad-brainstorming`; `bmad-customize`: script missing -> continue). `bmad-help` reads `{project-root}/_bmad/_config/bmad-help.csv` and has no stated behaviour when `_bmad/` is absent. Whether a model completes a session with no `_bmad/` at all is UNVERIFIED.
  - Source URL: https://registry.npmjs.org/bmad-method/-/bmad-method-6.12.4.tgz (`src/bmm-skills/plan/bmad-prd/SKILL.md:22`, `src/core-skills/bmad-brainstorming/SKILL.md:23`, `src/core-skills/bmad-customize/SKILL.md:17`, `src/core-skills/bmad-help/SKILL.md:25-26`)
  - Relevance: MEDIUM
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (R01 s7.4: in 6.9.0 no skill detects a missing `_bmad/`)

**Documented install flow for a new project**

- **Finding W02-9:** the 6.9.0 install guide (at the tag of this repo's pin) says: run `npx bmad-method install` in the project directory, or headless `install --yes --modules <list> --tools claude-code [--directory <path>]`; `--tools` is required for fresh `--yes` installs; `--directory` defaults to the working directory; a re-run where `_bmad/` exists defaults to quick-update under `--yes`; `--set <module>.<key>=<value>` is a post-install patch into `_bmad/config.toml` or `_bmad/config.user.toml` and `_bmad/<module>/config.yaml`; a single install calls the GitHub API once per external module, anonymous calls capped at 60 per hour per IP. The guide says nothing about an empty directory, a directory with no `.git`, a global install, `~/.claude`, or install on demand.
  - Source URL: https://github.com/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD/blob/v6.9.0/docs/how-to/install-bmad.md
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: supports (R01 s1, s3.2: flags and the quick-update default) and extends (documentation-level answer to GL G-07 on which files `--set` patches; documents the external-module rate limit behind GL G-12). The empty-directory and no-`.git` behaviour stay UNVERIFIED (GL G-10)
- **Finding W02-10:** the 6.9.0 getting-started tutorial says the installer "creates two folders: `_bmad/`... `_bmad-output/`" and sends the new user to the `bmad-help` skill after the install.
  - Source URL: https://github.com/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD/blob/v6.9.0/docs/tutorials/getting-started.md
  - Relevance: MEDIUM
  - Relationship to codebase: contradicts in part: the code also writes `.claude/skills/bmad-*` and an empty `docs/` (R01 s4). The tutorial is incomplete, not wrong; the code finding stands
- **Finding W02-11:** the install page on the main branch now documents the v7 preview alpha and says of the v6.12 flow "The rest of this page does not apply to v6.12"; v6.12 remains the stable line with the same project-level `npx bmad-method install`. A reader who follows "the documented install flow" on the website gets the v7 flow, not the one the pin uses; the 6.9.0 flow must be cited from the `v6.9.0` tag.
  - Source URL: https://docs.bmad-method.org/start/install-bmad/; https://github.com/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD/blob/main/docs/start/install-bmad.md
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: contradicts the assumption that "the documented install flow" is the v6 flow (an assumption in the option list, not a code finding); the repo's own `manifest.yaml:14` flow is the v6 one

**v7 preview, upstream issues, plugin channel**

- **Finding W02-12:** the v7 preview installs skills with the Skills CLI (`npx skills add bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD ...`, `-g` for a user directory, project scope by default) and creates the project's `_bmad/` through an explicit `bmad setup` step of the hub skill. Upstream's own design is offer-and-ask: `setup_check.py:67` returns only dependency notes when `_bmad/` is absent, and the "Offer to run `bmad setup`" messages fire only when `_bmad/` already exists but a module's questions or scripts are missing; a first setup runs when the user asks for it. It requires `uv`, and it is an alpha with breaking changes, not reachable by any `bmad-method@<version>` pin. It also defines `{project-root}` as the nearest folder containing `_bmad/`.
  - Source URL: https://github.com/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD/blob/main/docs/reference/skills-and-agents.md; https://github.com/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD/blob/main/skills/bmad/SKILL.md; https://github.com/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD/blob/main/skills/bmad/references/setup.md; https://github.com/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD/blob/main/skills/bmad/scripts/setup_check.py; https://github.com/vercel-labs/skills/blob/main/docs/commands.md
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (validates the shape of options B and D, "skills present" separate from "project runtime present", but not full automation: upstream asks first; the `main` branch has no `tools/installer/` directory, so the 6.x installer lives only in published 6.x tarballs and the `V6.12` branch)
- **Finding W02-13:** issue #2677 reports exactly the first-use gap: skills installed user-wide through the Claude Code plugin marketplace run `{project-root}/_bmad/scripts/resolve_customization.py`, a path "only the npx installer creates". The reporter counted 80 call sites at 6.10.0, 26 with a documented fallback; `resolve_config.py` "does not degrade" (exit 1 without `_bmad/config.toml`). The maintainer closed it as fixed by redesign (v7 flat `skills/` tree via `npx skills` and plugin marketplaces, installer removed); no first-use bootstrap was added to any 6.x release.
  - Source URL: https://github.com/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD/issues/2677
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: supports (R01 s7: `resolve_config.py` exits 1 and `resolve_customization.py` falls back; no skill detects a missing `_bmad/`), independent upstream evidence
- **Finding W02-14:** issue #2624 (open) raises a trust concern: every skill's first step executes a Python script located inside the project being worked on, and the skill cannot tell an installer-written resolver from one a repository committed; any mechanism that creates `_bmad/` in a repository should be explicit and visible.
  - Source URL: https://github.com/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD/issues/2624
  - Relevance: MEDIUM
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (an image-baked, version-pinned installer, as in this repo, answers it for our case, W02 recommendation 3)
- **Finding W02-15:** issue #1852 (a request for a global framework install) was closed as completed on 2026-04-29 with a workaround: install BMAD once in a workspace folder with the project folders underneath and launch the agent from the workspace. That is a variant of option E restricted to a workspace directory rather than `~`.
  - Source URL: https://github.com/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD/issues/1852
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (supports the owner's "never globally" stance as the upstream default; whether it works with this repo's layout depends on how 6.9.0 skills find `{project-root}`, and repositories with their own `.git` are UNVERIFIED, GL G-16)
- **Finding W02-16:** the 6.x tarballs include a Claude Code plugin channel (`.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`; in 6.9.0 two plugins at version 6.8.0 with 12 and 33 skills; in 6.12.4 one plugin with 28 skills). It installs user-wide skills in Claude Code's plugin cache and, per #2677, does not create `_bmad/`. It is therefore not a first-use solution; it leaves exactly the gap this task closes.
  - Source URL: https://registry.npmjs.org/bmad-method/-/bmad-method-6.9.0.tgz (`.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`); https://github.com/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD/issues/2677
  - Relevance: MEDIUM
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (whether this route counts as "user level" is the owner question in GL section 5 item 1; the skill counts do not settle the 46 versus 47 question, GL G-11)
- **Finding W02-17:** no upstream document or release note describes bootstrap-on-first-use or install-on-demand for 6.x. The only skill documented as standalone is `bmad-project-context` (6.11.0 and later: "it runs standalone with no BMad install"); it is not in the 6.9.0 skill set. A GitHub issue search for `global_target_dir` returned five platform-table pull requests and none about a consumer; the search API returns only the 10 to 12 most recently updated items per query, so older matches may exist.
  - Source URL: https://api.github.com/repos/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD/releases?per_page=30; https://github.com/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD/issues/2677
  - Relevance: HIGH
  - Relationship to codebase: supports (R01 s7.4: any first-use bootstrap has to come from outside BMAD)
- **Finding W02-18:** issue #3016 (fixed in 6.12.1) shows an upstream example of an incomplete first-use state producing a HALT: with `_bmad/` committed but the gitignored `config.user.toml` absent, `bmad-build` and `bmad-build-auto` stopped with "missing config value communication_language". Those skills are not in the 6.9.0 set.
  - Source URL: https://github.com/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD/issues/3016
  - Relevance: LOW (MEDIUM if the pin is bumped)
  - Relationship to codebase: extends (not a current risk for the 6.9.0 pin)

### 5.3 External Research Summary

External research answered all five questions the codebase left to web research (GL section 4 and G-01 to G-05) and closed none of them completely. Every external fact is context; the codebase findings remain the source of truth.

| Open question | External answer | Findings | Residual UNVERIFIED |
|---|---|---|---|
| G-01 / Q-1: are skills created after the session started loaded? | Split: an existing watched `.claude/skills/` picks them up live; a new top-level directory needs `/reload-skills` or a restart; a SessionStart hook can return `reloadSkills` | W01-3, W01-11 | Prompt-time rescan without the user; whether the behaviour holds at the pinned Claude Code 2.1.289 (`Dockerfile.coder:110`) |
| G-02 / Q-2: user-level skills in every project, and ranking | Yes for all projects on the machine; personal beats project on a name collision | W01-1, W01-2, W01-19 | Managed keys that would disable user-level skills and hooks in this image |
| G-03: drop-in hooks | Documented by inference, list-merged, additive to user hooks, not user-disableable | W01-15, W01-16, W01-17 | No single explicit sentence; `test-73` with a second file |
| G-04: hook and permission runtime | Live reload of hooks, stdout as context, timeouts 600 s and 30 s, trust hold-back in interactive sessions, default Bash prompt | W01-8 to W01-10, W01-13, W01-14, W01-18 | Held-back hook timing; display of SessionStart output; `skipDangerousModePermissionPrompt` |
| G-05 / Q-3: later upstream user-scope install | None through 6.12.4; 6.12.4 exists; the only user-scope pattern is the v7 preview, a different channel | W02-1 to W02-5, W02-11, W02-12 | Individual 6.11.0 and 6.12.0 to 6.12.3 tarballs, `-next`, `V6.12` branch |
| G-06 (part) bare `python3` steps | 6.12.4 uses `uv run` with no `python3 ` lines | W02-7 | Model emission and a fresh workspace's hook registration (codebase gap) |
| G-07, G-12, G-14 (part) | Documentation-level answers for `--set` targets and the external-module rate limit; release note #2671 on `--set` ordering | W02-6, W02-9 | Observation in CI |
| G-16 (part) option E | Upstream's declined-request workaround is an install at a parent workspace folder | W02-15 | Repos with their own `.git` |

What the external facts change for the option comparison (inputs for Section 6, not a recommendation):

- **Option F:** no code path through 6.12.4; a pin bump does not enable it; the v7 preview is a different product shape, channel and runtime layout (W02-2, W02-12).
- **Option D:** a SessionStart hook with `reloadSkills` is the only documented way to show new project skills in the same session; UserPromptSubmit has a 30 s default timeout and no rescan field; an interactive session holds the hook back until workspace trust is accepted; a managed (D2) hook cannot be switched off by the user; any user-level hook fires in every project (W01-8 to W01-11, W01-15, W01-16, W01-18).
- **Option B:** a stub must not reuse a `bmad-*` name (personal beats project); it runs the install through a permission prompt unless `allowed-tools` pre-approves it, and the user then needs `/reload-skills` or a restart because a new `.claude/skills/` directory is not watched; it is not gated by workspace trust (W01-2, W01-3, W01-6, W01-18).
- **Option C:** unaffected by Claude Code behaviour; it fires only when a `bmad` process runs (R02 s6.2, an inference marked UNVERIFIED there). It matters only for a new, separately named command, because `bmad install`, `--version` and `--help` must pass through the wrapper unchanged and the existing manual `bmad install` is the only mechanism found for the CLI reading [synthesis inference: the owner decides whether it meets the decision] (Stage 0 only, Section 7).
- **Option E:** upstream's own workaround is a parent-folder install; it conflicts with K-01 when the folder is HOME (W02-15).
- **Pin bump:** not needed for first-use creation; it would change the skill set and layout, move skills to `uv run`, make `--yes` without `--directory` install into the working directory, make deprecated names opt-in and require `uv` for Build variants (W02-6, W02-7).

Contradictions: none of the 19 web-01 findings is CODE-CONTRADICTED by a codebase file (W01 "Discrepancies"). Two web-02 items disagree with a codebase-adjacent statement and neither overturns a code finding: the 6.9.0 tutorial's "two folders" (W02-10) and the main-branch install page that documents v7 (W02-11). The two web files do not contradict each other. The web-02 draft sentence that 6.9.0 had no `config.user.toml` layer was corrected inside the file: the 6.9.0 installer code mentions `config.user.toml` 17 times in 5 files (W02 s5 item 2 (iii)).

Limits: WebFetch results are small-model summaries; the Coder image pins Claude Code 2.1.289 (`Dockerfile.coder:110`) and whether the docs hold at that version is not observed; community material was not read; the `reloadSkills` introduction version comes from a user-authored issue only. No external statement was used to describe the architecture of this repository.

## 6. Options Analysis

### 6.0 Basis of the options (read first)

Evidence tags used in this section:

| Tag | Meaning here |
|---|---|
| [CODE-VERIFIED] | Read in code, config or tests of this repo, or in the installed 6.9.0 package at `/opt/nvm/versions/node/v24.22.2/lib/node_modules/bmad-method/`, by a research agent. Nothing was executed: the research ran no `bmad install` and no test, so every test verdict is a reading of assertion code. |
| External context (code) | Shipped npm tarballs of `bmad-method` 6.9.0, 6.10.0 and 6.12.4, searched with Grep by web-02 (https://registry.npmjs.org/bmad-method/-/bmad-method-6.12.4.tgz and the same pattern for the other versions). |
| External context (docs) [UNVERIFIED - doc-only, no code confirmation] | Official Claude Code docs (web-01, https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills, /hooks, /settings, /managed-settings, /permissions) and upstream BMAD release notes and issues (web-02). Shown with their source. These facts are never the only basis of an effort rating, a risk rating or the recommendation; they appear as caveats and as questions for a live check. |
| [UNVERIFIED] | Not observed. Marked in the cell where it matters. |
| OBSERVED-FROM-6.10.0-TRACKED-TREE | Rows of file 01 section 8 come from this worktree's tracked 6.10.0 tree, not from 6.9.0 installer output. No rating or recommendation in this section rests on them. |

Rating key (this report's own rubric, applied to counted facts from the research; it is not a research finding):

| Rating | Rule |
|---|---|
| Effort XS / S / M / L / XL | XS = 1 to 2 product files, no pinned-test change. S = 3 to 4 files, or exactly one amended or new test. M = 5 to 7 files, or 2 to 3 amended or new tests. L = 8 or more files, or two delivery layers (image and Terraform). XL = needs a change outside this repo. Files are counted without the decision-record documents that every option needs (see the common-facts table, row 12). |
| Risk Low / Medium / High | Count the factors that apply. **R1** a certain pinned-test break. **R2** a new persistent artifact in HOME. **R3** a shared file with other writers, or a shared block that other tests compare. **R4** image-wide behaviour not gated by the `bmad` slug or the profile. **R5** adds an image layer against the CI gate of 70 layers (68 used, 2 free). 0 factors = Low, 1 = Medium, 2 or more = High. |

Facts common to every option (they are stated once here and not repeated in each option):

| # | Fact | Source |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The 2026-11-07 owner decision: BMAD should create its needed directories on first use in a project that never had it; global installation of BMAD is not requested; OD-1 is superseded for first-use creation only. The wording exists only at `BUILD-REQUEST.md:14` (no copy in the specs yet). | BUILD-REQUEST.md:14; research-notes F9-1; gaps G-26 |
| 2 | 6.9.0 has no first-use bootstrap. Skills need a project-local `_bmad/`. `resolve_config.py` exits 1 when `_bmad/config.toml` is absent; `resolve_customization.py` silently falls back to skill defaults; no skill detects a missing `_bmad/` or tells the user to install. Any bootstrap must therefore come from outside BMAD. [CODE-VERIFIED] | 01 section 7 and its Key Takeaways (`resolve_config.py:156`, `resolve_customization.py:209-236`) |
| 3 | Non-interactive per-project install: `NPM_CONFIG_OFFLINE=true bmad install --yes --directory "$P" --modules bmm --tools claude-code --output-folder _bmad-output --user-name "<name>"`. `--output-folder _bmad-output` must stay whenever `--user-name` is passed (mechanism at `official-modules.js:1409`; outcome of dropping it UNVERIFIED). It writes only inside `$P`: `_bmad/`, `_bmad-output/{planning,implementation}-artifacts/`, `docs/`, `.claude/skills/bmad-*`; no git call, no `~/.claude` write, no HOME write on the bmm-only path (by code, never observed). [CODE-VERIFIED] | 01 sections 3.2, 3.2a, 3.4, 4, 5; 03 A2 |
| 4 | One trigger predicate, FIRST_USE(P): P is the git work-tree top (else the current directory); `$P/_bmad` does not exist; no `$P/.claude/skills/bmad-*` entry on disk (tracked or untracked); `$P` writable. It widens `catalogue/bmad/install.sh:24` and drops the `.git` requirement of `install.sh:22`. A directory with no `.git` ends in "nothing to configure" in the slug as written, so the slug's branch order cannot be reused for it. Whether `bmad install` itself works with no `.git` is UNVERIFIED (R-3). | 03 A2 |

Only framework content is per project, so decision text must say "no global or user-level install of BMad framework content". [CODE-VERIFIED] | 02 section 1; 05 b nuance, C1 |
| 6 | Health and readiness are blind to any project except the recorded seed repo: `spec2_health_observed bmad` reads only `<seed>/_bmad/_config/manifest.yaml` or tracked `.claude/skills/bmad-*` (`spec2-health.sh:99-114`). No option changes the state `Installed-not-Configured` for a seedless HOME unless `spec2-health.sh` and the `record_health` rows of test-57 change together. Marker `~/.aidev/installed/bmad` is never removed on deselection (`startup.sh:1116-1117`). [CODE-VERIFIED] | 02 section 2; 05 open question 1 |

If `_bmad/` exists (even a half-written one), `--yes` takes quick-update (`ui.js:233-262`, `existing-install.js:64-66, 111`). Prior-review observation, not re-run: 6.9.0 over a 6.10.0 tree downgraded the manifest and changed 41 files with exit code 0. A failed first run leaves a partial `_bmad/` (mechanism CODE-VERIFIED; what quick-update does to it UNVERIFIED). FIRST_USE(P) is the only barrier, and model compliance with a stub's text is UNVERIFIED. | 01 F5-D, F5-F, F5-8; gaps G-08, G-13 |

Option list. B and D are split into variants because the research shows they differ on the criteria that decide (file 02 section 6; file 05 F1.4 reads "B" and "D" in file 04 section 7 as B1 and D1).

### Option A: Document only (keep the manual one-command install and say so in README)

**Description.** No behaviour change. The README and the baked welcome text state that BMad framework content is created per project, at boot for the seed repo only, and in any other project by one manual command. Nothing is created on first use.

**How it works (technical).**

| Step | What happens | Source |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The user runs, from the project root, `bmad install --yes --directory . --modules bmm --tools claude-code --output-folder _bmad-output`. All flags exist in 6.9.0 (`commands/install.js:14-37`) [CODE-VERIFIED]. That it completes end to end is UNVERIFIED (R-11). | `catalogue/bmad/manifest.yaml:12-14`; 05 d4 |
| 2 | The command differs from `install.sh:42` in three ways: no `NPM_CONFIG_OFFLINE=true`, no `--user-name`, no `info/exclude` lines, so in another repo the new directories appear as untracked files. | 02 section 3.1 |
| 3 | `templates/VSCode-AIDev02/README.md` is outside the image build context (`main.tf:119`), so a workspace user cannot read it; the README must itself carry the command, and the baked welcome text must keep it too. | 05 (e) |
| 4 | README line 241 (the Default profile cell) is reconciled in place: five cells, no raw pipe, lines 96 to 101 untouched (anchor `upstream GitHub CLI release v` at `README.md:100`, `releases.json:113`); the file is linted (`.markdownlint-cli2.yaml`). | 05 (e) |
| 5 | A welcome-text edit must keep the tokens `bmad` and `bmad --help` (`test-19:517-529`), must not add a column-0 `version:` line (Renovate row `expected.txt:8`), must not add lifecycle strings (`test-49:326`), and retags the image because `catalogue_sha256` hashes the catalogue (`main.tf:74`). | 05 (a-supplement), (f); 04 section 5 |

| Row | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Effort | **XS.** 1 required file (`templates/VSCode-AIDev02/README.md`, outside `image/`, no retag); the welcome text in `image/catalogue/bmad/manifest.yaml` is an optional second file and does retag. No pinned-test change. |
| Risk | **Low.** 0 of R1 to R5. Only conditional break: `test-19` if the welcome text loses `bmad` or `bmad --help`. |
| Reuse of existing code | The existing welcome text and manual command (`manifest.yaml:12-14`). |
| Files/systems affected | `templates/VSCode-AIDev02/README.md:241`; optionally `templates/VSCode-AIDev02/image/catalogue/bmad/manifest.yaml:12-14`; `CHANGELOG.md`; the decision-record documents of common fact 12. |
| Pros | No code, no test change, no image layer; keeps the "no global install" part intact; it is also the documentation half that every other option still needs, because the mechanism may not fire (05 b3 row `manifest.yaml:12-14`). |
| Cons | Creates nothing on first use itself. Under reading (1) of "first use" (running a `bmad` CLI command) the only mechanism found is the existing manual `bmad install`, which creates the directories only when the user runs it [synthesis inference: whether that meets the 2026-11-07 decision is the owner's call, Section 9 row 2]. Under the slash-skill and session-open readings it fails the 2026-11-07 decision (research-notes SOLUTION_RESEARCH row A; research-notes F9-3 R9: the precondition for "documentation only" is met but its scope is superseded because the decision asks for behaviour). The existing welcome text reads "If this workspace has a seed repo, BMad is already set up there" (`manifest.yaml:12-13`): conditional on a seed, but not on the install guards (`install.sh:22-25`) or a failed install, so "already set up there" can be false (02 C7). |

### Option B: User-level bootstrap skill or command under `~/.claude/skills/`

**Description.** A content-free stub skill (no BMAD material) is placed in `~/.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md`. When it is invoked in a project, its text tells Claude to evaluate FIRST_USE(P) and, if true, run the pinned offline install for that project. Three delivery routes are described in file 02 section 6.1.

**How it works (technical).**

| Step | What happens | Source |
|---|---|---|
| 1 Delivery | B1: a new step in `catalogue/bmad/install.sh` between line 55 and the marker write at line 58; the stub text must be an inline heredoc because a fourth file in `catalogue/bmad/` breaks the three-file rule (`test-1-catalogue-shape.sh:25, 43-67`). B2: a source file COPY-ed into the image plus a `seed_if_absent` line in `startup.sh` section 6 (pattern `startup.sh:275`; zero-layer form: ride the existing COPY to `/usr/local/lib/aidev/` at `Dockerfile.coder:547-548` and seed from there as `startup.sh:291-293` does; allowed by `test-60:87-88`; the fold itself is UNVERIFIED, U18). B3: a new `resource "coder_script"` in `modules.tf`, count-gated on `contains(local.active_slugs, "bmad")`, pattern `coder_script "orca_retirement"` (`modules.tf:82-109`). | 02 section 6.1; 02 A5; 04 A.3 (a) |
| 2 Invocation | The user invokes the stub by its slash name in a project that has no BMAD. | 03 A3 B1 to B3 step 1 |
| 3 Predicate and install | The stub's text has Claude run FIRST_USE(P) and then the common install command in a Bash tool call; the user sees the installer output and the box "BMAD is ready to use!" (`installer.js:1219-1243`). Whether Claude follows the text is UNVERIFIED (UV-17). | 03 A3 shared facts, B steps 2 and 3 |
| 4 Skills appear | `P/.claude/skills/bmad-*` exist after the install. The installer's own text says to launch the agent from the project folder afterwards (`installer.js:1233`). | 03 A3 B step 4 |
| 5 Naming | The name must avoid the whole words `preview`, `share`, `share-port`, `expose`, `dev-preview` in a `cp` or `ln -s` destination (`test-3-reserved-names.sh:27, 55`); it must not equal a name superclaude installs (39 packaged names, none starting with `bmad`) or the three Orca names. | 04 A.2; 03 section 5 |

Delivery variants of B:

| Variant | Rebuild class | What persists in HOME | Layers | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | image rebuild (catalogue hash, `main.tf:74`); effective on the next agent start after `coder update` | `~/.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md`, rewritten every boot unless written copy-if-absent | 0 (`COPY ... catalogue /catalogue`, `Dockerfile.coder:593`) | 02 6.1, A5 |
| B2 | image rebuild (new source in `image_input_files`, `main.tf:80-101`; `Dockerfile.coder` edit) | same path, created once, then user-owned; a later image update never reaches an existing workspace (`startup.sh:245-248`) | +1 as written (own COPY); 0 if folded (UNVERIFIED, U18) | 02 6.1, A5; 03 M1 |
| B3 | template push for `modules.tf`, which is outside the image hash (`main.tf:104-107`); as planned, step 4B.6 also edits `catalogue/bmad/manifest.yaml`, which changes `catalogue_sha256` (`main.tf:74`), so the image is retagged and needs `coder update`; runs at the next agent start after the workspace is updated | same path, rewritten each agent start | 0 | 02 6.1, 6.4 |

| Row | B1 | B2 | B3 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Effort | **S.** 2 to 3 files: `catalogue/bmad/install.sh`, optionally `manifest.yaml`, and one amended test (`test-57-catalogue-configure.sh:450` renamed to `no seed: bmad writes only the first-use skill` with one pinned file hash, 04 A.11). | **M.** 6 files: `Dockerfile.coder`, `main.tf` (`image_input_files`), a new non-hidden source under `workspace_defaults/`, `startup.sh` section 6, and two test lists (`test-startup-boot.sh:2775`, `test-22-aienv-fresh-workspace.sh:271-272`, 04 A.11). | **L** as planned in Section 8 (S on the researched minimum, where `modules.tf` alone is a template push). Product files: `modules.tf`, plus `manifest.yaml` and `README.md` for the user-facing text. Two delivery layers: `modules.tf` is a template push, and `manifest.yaml` sits under `image/catalogue/`, which `main.tf:74` hashes into `catalogue_sha256`, so step 4B.6 retags the image and needs `coder update` (the files and tests alone would be M: 6 files, 2 new tests; two delivery layers is L). Tests: one new static pin next to `test-52-browser-usability-evidence.sh:1374-1377` (04 A.11, step 5.2) and the new first-use execution test with its driver-table row in `coder-ci-aidev02-contract.yml` (step 5.3), so 2 new tests (M = 2 to 3 amended or new tests); optionally a ledger kind at `test-startup-boot.sh:232`. |
| Risk | **High.** R1 (`test-57:450`, certain) and R2 (HOME artifact rewritten at every boot, including the no-seed boot). | **High.** R2 (HOME artifact), R4 (section 6 is not gated by the `bmad` slug or the profile; it also runs for `minimal`) and, as written, R5 (68 to 69 layers). Still High (R2 and R4) if the zero-layer fold holds (UNVERIFIED, U18). | **Medium.** R2 only. No certain break, no layer (the catalogue COPY already exists, `Dockerfile.coder:593`); the only rebuild is the retag from the welcome-text edit of step 4B.6 (see Effort). The conditional breaks (S16c, S18, S19 of file 05) are avoided by placement: a separate resource after `modules.tf:109`, no new `locals` block above `modules.tf:16`, a display name other than `aidev-startup`. |
| Reuse of existing code | `install.sh` guard, exclude and status pattern (`install.sh:21-55`); the common install command. | `seed_if_absent` (`startup.sh:201-274`) and the config-seeding precedent (`startup.sh:291-293`, `Dockerfile.coder:543-545`). | `coder_script "orca_retirement"` (`modules.tf:82-109`); `jq` is in the image (`Dockerfile.coder:590`). |
| Files/systems affected | `templates/VSCode-AIDev02/image/catalogue/bmad/install.sh`; `.github/contract-tests/test-57-catalogue-configure.sh`. | `image/Dockerfile.coder`, `main.tf`, `image/scripts/startup.sh`, `image/workspace_defaults/`, two contract tests. | `templates/VSCode-AIDev02/modules.tf`; `.github/contract-tests/test-52-browser-usability-evidence.sh`. |
| Pros | One file in the slug that already owns BMad; updates reach existing workspaces each boot. | Not a slug-gated code path; the file is user-owned afterwards. | Lowest risk of the three (no certain break, 0 layers); `modules.tf` itself needs only a template push (the welcome-text edit of step 4B.6 adds a catalogue-hash retag, see Effort); refreshed on every start; profile-gated by `active_slugs`. |
| Cons | Certain break of the only "writes nothing" oracle; a heredoc body must carry no unquoted install verb (`test-19:208-209`); the stub must also be written when no seed is recorded, which is exactly the case `test-57:446-450` pins (gaps D-12). | Copy-if-absent means a fixed text for the life of the workspace; section 6 also runs for `minimal`, where bmad is absent; six pins to keep (test-67, test-7, test-60, test-3, test-49, test-59), and delivery is unproven by the live boot gate until `test-startup-boot.sh:2775` is extended. | Splits BMad logic between `modules.tf` and the catalogue slug; the catalogue README describes `module.tf` as `coder_app` plus `variable agent_id` and is silent on `coder_script` (02 6.1); no ledger row, so the live boot gate cannot see it; the bmad marker is never removed on deselection, so a user-level stub stays after switching to `minimal` unless cleanup is added (02 6.1, gaps D-5). |

**Shared points for B.** A user-level artifact is owner question 1 (section 7). Whether Claude Code loads a stub from `~/.claude/skills/` in every project, how it ranks it against a same-named project skill, and whether skills created during a running session appear are Claude Code behaviours that only external docs answer: user skills are listed for "All your projects", "Enterprise over personal, and personal over project", and a top-level `.claude/skills/` that did not exist at session start is not watched and needs `/reload-skills` (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills). [UNVERIFIED - doc-only, no code confirmation; the image pins Claude Code 2.1.289 at `Dockerfile.coder:110`, and whether the docs hold at that version is not observed.] If the docs hold, the stub must not reuse a `bmad-*` name. `superclaude install --force` leaves a skill name outside its packaged set alone: skills are replaced per packaged name only and the only `rmtree` calls are `install_skills.py:97` and `install_skill.py:48` [CODE-VERIFIED, 03 section 5].

### Option C: Image-level `bmad` CLI wrapper change or a new wrapper command

**Description.** The root-owned wrapper `/usr/local/bin/bmad` (today a pure `exec`) or a new command name next to it evaluates FIRST_USE(P) for the current project and runs the common install command before doing what the user asked.

**How it works (technical).**

| Step | What happens | Source |
|---|---|---|
| 1 Place | In place: the `{ echo "#!/bin/sh"; echo "exec ..." } > /usr/local/bin/bmad` group at `Dockerfile.coder:142`, inside the root layer at lines 136 to 145 (0 new layers). Late-COPY helper: a separate script added to the existing `bin/` COPY at `Dockerfile.coder:492-493` or the `/usr/local/lib/aidev/` COPY at `:469` (0 layers if it rides them, UNVERIFIED, U19; +1 as its own COPY). | 02 section 6.2, A5 |
| 2 What may be intercepted | The wrapper must pass `bmad --version`, `bmad --help` and `bmad install ...` through unchanged: `test-19:1175-1190` and `545-561` run `bmad --version` and `timeout 10s bmad --help </dev/null`; `install.sh:18, 42` and the health probe `manifest.yaml:25` use them. The 6.9.0 subcommands are `install`, `status`, `uninstall` (`tools/installer/commands/`). So the only subcommands left to act on are `status`, `uninstall` or a new command name; acting on `status` or `uninstall` would run an install before a command that does not install (`bmad uninstall` in a never-BMAD project would install and then uninstall), so this report does not use them, and the one place C can add anything is a new, separately named command. This is a consequence of the cited constraints, not a research statement. | 02 section 6.2; 03 A3 C.5; 04 section 4 |
| 3 Trigger limit | It fires only when something executes `bmad`. It cannot fire when a BMAD skill is invoked in Claude Code, because no `bmad` process runs then (inference, UNVERIFIED, UV-28). | 02 section 6.2; 03 A3 C.5 |
| 4 Seeded runs | The slug's own call `bmad install ...` (`install.sh:42`) goes through this wrapper in every seeded test-57 run, so the wrapper is exercised by `test-57` at lines 173-178, 323-324 and 457-459 (the second run at 331-336 hits the `_bmad` guard and does not call it). | 05 F1.1, X2 |
| 5 Pin strings | The literal `npm install -g bmad-method@X.Y.Z` must stay in `Dockerfile.coder` (`expected.txt:6`, `renovate-global.json5:48`). | 05 (f) |

| Row | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Effort | **S.** 2 to 3 files: `image/Dockerfile.coder` (one RUN group), optionally `catalogue/bmad/manifest.yaml` so a new command name is discoverable (`manifest.yaml:12-14`), and, only if the wrapper moves to a `printf '%s\n' \` block, one extraction check beside `test-60-llm-gateway-env.sh:162-168` (04 A.11). **S** on the researched minimum; **M** as planned in Section 8 for a new command name, because steps 5.3 and 5.4 add the first-use execution test and the wrapper pass-through case (2 new tests). |
| Risk | **Medium.** R4 only: the wrapper is baked for every profile, so it changes what `bmad <anything>` means in `minimal` workspaces too (02 section 6.2 "Stretches"). No certain break. Conditional breaks: `test-19:1175-1190, 545-561` (wrapper not an executable file or `--version`/`--help` non-zero); `test-57` seeded runs (`323-324, 457-459`, budget `173-183`); `test-60:162-169` if the `claude` printf block is touched; `test-71` if a second `CG_VERSION=` line appears; `test-67` for an unlisted new COPY source; `test-3` for a file-based wrapper using a reserved word; `test-49` for lifecycle strings. |
| Reuse of existing code | The existing wrapper (`Dockerfile.coder:134-143`) and the install command of `install.sh:42`. |
| Files/systems affected | `templates/VSCode-AIDev02/image/Dockerfile.coder:136-145`; optionally `image/catalogue/bmad/manifest.yaml`; `main.tf` only for a new COPY source. Nothing in HOME. |
| Pros | No user-level artifact, so owner question 1 does not arise for it; no certain pinned-test break; writes nothing under HOME at boot (the artifact is image-level); 0 layers in place. |
| Cons | Only the "running a `bmad` CLI command" reading; the commands a wrapper may intercept are few (step 2), and `bmad install`, which already creates the directories, must stay untouched; image rebuild; the Dockerfile comment at `Dockerfile.coder:133-135` explains why the wrapper is a file and not a symlink (the bin is a `#!/usr/bin/env node` script and NVM node is not on the default PATH), so it would need one added line saying the wrapper now also runs the first-use block (comment text re-read in the code, `Dockerfile.coder:133-135`). |

### Option D: Claude Code user-level or managed hook (SessionStart or UserPromptSubmit)

**Description.** A hook that evaluates FIRST_USE(P) cheaply on every session start (or every prompt) and, when true, runs the common install command. Three delivery routes: D1 a hook entry merged into `~/.claude/settings.json` by `catalogue/bmad/install.sh` (model: `ironclaude/install.sh:100-136`); D2 a drop-in file under `image/etc/claude-code/managed-settings.d/` (existing file `10-retention.json` holds `{"cleanupPeriodDays": 90}`; COPY pattern `Dockerfile.coder:500-501`; listed at `main.tf:87`); D3 the same merge done by a root `coder_script` in `modules.tf`.

**How it works (technical).**

| Step | What happens | Source |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `~/.claude/settings.json` in this workspace already holds SessionStart (5), UserPromptSubmit (3) and PreToolUse (3) registrations; the 29 existing `timeout` values are 1 to 10 s, while install time inside a workspace is unknown (UNVERIFIED, U1; CI `cold bmad` was 374 to 392 ms offline, the memlog "about 4s" includes an npx fetch, and the two must not be blended). | 03 section 4, A3 D.2; 01 F5-C, F5-E |
| 2 | A hook that waits for the install needs a far larger `timeout` than any existing hook, or must run in the background. | 03 A3 D.2 |
| 3 | The skills are created after the session has started. Whether the session sees them is a Claude Code question (below). | 03 A3 D.3 |
| 4 | D1 and D3 write `~/.claude/settings.json`: IronClaude treats any hook command containing `/.claude/hooks/` as its own (`ironclaude/install.sh:15-16, 25`; `spec2-health.sh:90-91`; `test-57:140`), so a bmad hook command with that substring would make the IronClaude `hook` observation true without IronClaude; there is no lock against the other `settings.json` writers (`ironclaude/install.sh:139` comment); `superclaude install` keeps a hook with a different command string, and `ironclaude/install.sh:104-136` fails the ironclaude slug if a pre-existing hook command disappears. | 02 section 6.3; 03 section 5 |
| 5 | D2 avoids HOME, but `test-73-claude-retention.sh:102-114` requires exactly one COPY line containing the `10-retention.json` path, in the exact form `COPY --chown=0:0 --chmod=0644 <rel> /<rel>`, and one RUN line holding both `/etc/claude-code` directories; a second drop-in needs its own COPY line (+1 layer), no `cleanupPeriodDays` key, and valid JSON. | 02 A5; 04 A.6 |
| 6 | Blast radius: the hook acts in every project, so "opening a session" would install in every repo lacking BMAD ("install everywhere", not "first use of BMAD"); no user prompt in the path. | 03 A3 D.1, D.5; research-notes row D |
| 7 | External context [UNVERIFIED - doc-only, no code confirmation] (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks, /settings, /managed-settings): hook timeout defaults are 600 s for SessionStart and 30 s for UserPromptSubmit; a SessionStart hook may return `reloadSkills: true` so freshly written skills are visible in the same session; no re-scan field was found for UserPromptSubmit; in an interactive session hooks from every settings file, including `~/.claude/settings.json`, are held back until the folder's workspace trust is accepted (whether a held-back SessionStart hook then runs late is not stated); hook entries merge across user, project, local and managed levels and a managed hook cannot be disabled by the user; that a drop-in may hold `hooks` is documented only by inference. | web-01 sections 2.3, 2.4, 3.2, 3.3, 4.1 |

Researched minimum: 4 files, new `image/etc/claude-code/managed-settings.d/20-*.json`, `Dockerfile.coder` (own COPY), `main.tf:80-101`, and a second-file check in `test-73-claude-retention.sh` (04 A.11). As planned the count is 8 files: those four, `catalogue/bmad/manifest.yaml` and `README.md` for the user-facing text (step 4D.7), the new first-use execution test (step 5.3) and its driver-table row in `coder-ci-aidev02-contract.yml` (step 5.3); the tests are that new test and the amended `test-73` (step 5.4), which is 2 (M by tests), and 8 or more files is L. One delivery layer (image). | **L** as planned in Section 8 (S on the researched minimum, where `modules.tf` alone is a template push). `modules.tf` (jq merge in a new resource), `manifest.yaml` and `README.md` for the user-facing text, a static block pin next to `test-52:1374-1377` (step 5.2), the first-use execution test (step 5.3) and merge fixtures (step 5.4), so 3 new tests; optionally a ledger kind. ### Option E: `bmad install --directory ~` (home-level install)

**Description.** Run the installer once with the home directory as target.

**How it works (technical).** `--directory ~` passes `validateDirectorySync` (`ui.js:1414-1463`) and `~` is expanded (`ui.js:1568-1581`); `findBmadDir` returns `<dir>/_bmad`; the claude-code target is project-relative (`.claude/skills`). From the code the write set would be `~/_bmad/`, `~/_bmad-output/{planning,implementation}-artifacts/`, `~/docs/` and `~/.claude/skills/bmad-*` (01 section 6 item 2; UNVERIFIED, nothing was run; whether it edits `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` is UNVERIFIED, U-F1-1). The ancestor-conflict check is dead configuration in 6.9.0 (no platform sets `ancestor_conflict_check`, `_config-driven.js:191`, `platform-codes.yaml:10`), so the installer would not block it and would not block a later per-project install [CODE-VERIFIED]. It does not create the project's own `_bmad/`, which the skills read through `{project-root}/_bmad/...` where `{project-root}` is the project working directory (01 section 7.4), and `resolve_config.py` exits 1 without `<root>/_bmad/config.toml` (01 section 7.1). Note an internal nuance: 01 section 6 item 3 says the root walk stops at the project, while 01 section 7.2 shows `resolve_customization.py` starts from the skill's own directory first; both are code reads, neither was run, and neither changes the rating because E already fails the "no global install" criterion. Variant E2 (install into a workspace parent folder) is the upstream maintainer's workaround for a requested global install (issue #1852, https://github.com/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD/issues/1852, external context, doc-only); whether it works for repositories with their own `.git` is UNVERIFIED because `find_project_root` stops at the nearest `_bmad` or `.git` (web-02 recommendation 4).

| Row | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Effort | **S.** No repo file if the user runs it by hand; if run from `install.sh`, 3 files (`install.sh`, `test-57`, the 75 s boot budget at `test-startup-boot.sh:2129-2156`). It delivers nothing useful (see Cons), so the effort buys no result. |
| Risk | **High.** R1 (`test-57:450` certain if run from `install.sh`; the E write set is UNVERIFIED) and R2 (HOME). Also a global-style install of framework content, which the owner declined. |
| Reuse of existing code | None in the repo; the installer's `--directory` option. |
| Files/systems affected | `~/_bmad`, `~/_bmad-output`, `~/docs`, `~/.claude/skills` (by code reading); `test-57`. |
| Pros | No image change if user-run; the installer does not refuse it. |
| Cons | Violates "no global BMAD install"; leaves the project's `_bmad/` absent, so first-use creation is not achieved; puts `docs/` and `_bmad-output/` under HOME; `~/.claude/skills/bmad-*` become visible in every project (doc-only). |

### Option F: Upstream user-scope install through `global_target_dir`

**Description.** Use the `global_target_dir: ~/.claude/skills` value that `platform-codes.yaml` declares for `claude-code`.

**How it works (technical).** It does not. `global_target_dir` appears in exactly one file, `tools/installer/ide/platform-codes.yaml` (43 hits: comment lines 9 and 12, 41 value lines); no `.js`, `.json` or `.md` file reads it; the skill write path is `path.join(projectDir, target_dir)` (`_config-driven.js:245-248`); `commands/install.js:12-51` has no `--global`, `--user` or scope option [CODE-VERIFIED, 01 sections 2 and 5]. External context (code): the same token appears only in `platform-codes.yaml` in 6.10.0 (43 hits) and 6.12.4 (46 hits), and the 6.12.4 `install` options add only `--shims` and `--no-shims` (web-02 section 2); upstream added the field as platform data for parity with the Skills CLI table (issue #2313, doc-only). So a newer pin does not enable F. The only upstream user-scope mechanism is the v7 preview (`npx skills add -g` plus a user-triggered `bmad setup`), a different distribution channel, an alpha, and a different skill set and layout (web-02 section 8, doc-only); it is not reachable by any `bmad-method@<version>` pin. File 04's "X" marks for F rest on the question's wording and are void; file 01's tag governs (gaps G-25).

| Row | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Effort | **XL.** It needs an upstream feature that no published 6.x version has (a change outside this repo), plus a pin change that touches three Renovate-visible strings (`Dockerfile.coder:139`, `install.sh:18`, `manifest.yaml:2`). |
| Risk | **High.** Not implementable in 6.9.0; adopting the v7 line would be a new product shape needing its own plan, tests and an owner reading of OD-1 (web-02 section 8). |
| Reuse of existing code | None. |
| Files/systems affected | Upstream `bmad-method`; `Dockerfile.coder:139`, `catalogue/bmad/install.sh`, `manifest.yaml` if the pin moves. |
| Pros | None for this task. |
| Cons | No code path in 6.9.0, 6.10.0 or 6.12.4; a version bump also changes the skill set and layout and makes `--yes` without `--directory` install into the working directory (web-02 sections 3, 5), so bumping would need its own plan. |

### Option G: Instruction-text channel (a BMad block in `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`)

**Description.** Research found this as an alternative to a skill (02 section 6.1, last paragraph) but did not rate it. A marked block of instruction text, synced into `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` at every boot, tells Claude to run the common install command when FIRST_USE(P) holds.

**How it works (technical).** `aidev_claude_md_block_sync` re-syncs a marked block from `/etc/skel/.claude/CLAUDE.md` into `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` every boot (`startup.sh:1282-1285`); its markers are hard-coded to `SUPERCLAUDE_CODEGRAPH_BEGIN/END` (`claude-md-block.sh:23`), so a second marker pair or a generalised function is needed; `workspace_defaults/CLAUDE.md` (the seed source, `Dockerfile.coder:496`) has no BMad text today [CODE-VERIFIED]. Any BMad text outside the synced pair breaks the fresh-home equality `cmp /etc/skel/.claude/CLAUDE.md $HOME/.claude/CLAUDE.md` unless that assertion is amended (`test-startup-boot.sh:2083-2087, 3030-3037`); `test-74-claude-md-block.sh` fails if a second pair reuses the `SUPERCLAUDE_CODEGRAPH` markers (04 A.4, A.6). That Claude reads this file in every project and obeys it is Claude Code and model behaviour: UNVERIFIED.

| Row | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Effort | **M.** 5 files: `workspace_defaults/CLAUDE.md`, `scripts/lib/claude-md-block.sh`, `startup.sh` (second sync call), `test-74-claude-md-block.sh`, `test-startup-boot.sh` (two assertions). |
| Risk | **High.** R2 (`~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` in HOME) and R3 (the file is shared with IronClaude's block, saved and restored byte-for-byte around `superclaude install`, `ironclaude/install.sh:44-86`, and compared by two tests). |
| Reuse of existing code | `aidev_claude_md_block_sync` and the seed file. |
| Files/systems affected | As listed above; `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`. |
| Pros | No skill discovery question; survives reboot because the block is re-synced each boot. |
| Cons | Pure instruction text with no enforcement; breaks or forces amendment of two existing equality checks; loads into every session of every project; rated at low depth because the research evaluated it only in passing. |

### Option H: Upstream Claude Code plugin marketplace route

**Description.** BMAD ships a marketplace file for Claude Code plugins. External context (code and issues), web-02 section 9 item 7 and issue #2677 (https://github.com/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD/issues/2677, doc-only).

**How it works (technical).** The 6.9.0 tarball contains `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` with name `bmad-method` and 2 plugins (`bmad-pro-skills` 6.8.0, 12 skills; `bmad-method-lifecycle` 6.8.0, 33 skills); the 6.12.4 file has 1 plugin (28 skills). The route installs skills in Claude Code's plugin cache, and per the upstream issue it does not create `_bmad/`, so it leaves exactly the first-use gap open; upstream closed that issue as "fixed-by-redesign" (v7), not by a 6.x patch.

| Row | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Effort | **L.** No repo mechanism exists for it: it would need a settings merge (`enabledPlugins` and `extraKnownMarketplaces`, which superclaude fills only where absent, 03 section 5) and a plugin cache in HOME, in addition to a first-use step. Assessed from web evidence only. |
| Risk | **High.** R2 (plugin cache in HOME) and R3 (shared `settings.json`); the plugin versions (6.8.0) differ from the image pin 6.9.0; it puts BMAD framework skills at user level, which the owner declined. |
| Reuse of existing code | None found. |
| Files/systems affected | `~/.claude/settings.json`, `~/.claude/plugins/cache/` (reporter's output in the issue). |
| Pros | An upstream-supported way to get user-wide skills. |
| Cons | Not a first-use mechanism by itself; user-level BMAD content; version skew; needs network (UNVERIFIED). |

### Option I: Image-level managed ("enterprise") skill location

**Description.** Research found, in a docs table only, a managed location for skills. Same stub as Option B, but placed outside HOME. Not evaluated by any research file.

**How it works (technical).** The skills page lists an enterprise location described as "a `.claude/skills/` in the managed settings directory", with precedence "Enterprise over personal, and personal over project" (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills, web-01 section 1.1). The page states the location twice: the Enterprise row is `.claude/skills/<skill-name>/SKILL.md` in the managed settings directory, and the "Remove a skill" example is `/etc/claude-code/.claude/skills/<skill-name>/` on Linux (page re-fetched during Gate 3 fix cycle 1; the fetch is a small-model summary of the page). `/etc/claude-code/` is also the Linux managed directory of https://code.claude.com/docs/en/managed-settings. [UNVERIFIED - doc-only, no code confirmation; the documented path was not observed in the image.] In this repo the managed directory is root-owned and image-baked (`image/etc/claude-code/managed-settings.d/`, `Dockerfile.coder:473-475, 498-501`); each COPY has one fixed destination, so a different destination costs its own COPY (02 A5). The documented path `/etc/claude-code/.claude/skills/<skill-name>/` also needs its parent directories at 0755; the existing `install -d` RUN at `Dockerfile.coder:473-475` creates only `/etc/claude-code` and `/etc/claude-code/managed-settings.d` (its comment says one RUN, to avoid BuildKit auto-parent mode issues), and `test-73:102-114` pins the form of that RUN, so an edit to it has to be checked against `test-73` (not run).

| Row | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Effort | **M.** About 5 files by analogy with B2 and D2: new skill file, `Dockerfile.coder`, `main.tf:80-101`, a delivery test, `test-67` coverage. Assessed by analogy, not from a research file. |
| Risk | **High.** R4 (image-wide, not profile-gated) and R5 (+1 layer); precedence over every other level would let it shadow real BMAD skills [doc-only]. |
| Reuse of existing code | The D2 managed-directory COPY pattern. |
| Files/systems affected | `image/etc/claude-code/.claude/skills/<skill-name>/` (documented path, doc-only), `Dockerfile.coder` (COPY and the `install -d` RUN at `:473-475`), `main.tf`. |
| Pros | Nothing in HOME, so it avoids the "user level" wording of owner question 1 (whether managed counts is itself an owner reading). |
| Cons | Rests only on a docs table; unevaluated by the codebase research; image-wide. |
