## 7. Recommendation

### Recommended approach

The evidence ranks the options but cannot choose the trigger, because the trigger depends on two owner readings that no code or document settles (is a user-level stub allowed; what counts as first use). The recommendation is therefore an explicit sequence: Stage 0 does not wait for the owner (its item (c) states plan defaults that the owner can still change, Section 9 rows 5, 6 and 10); Stages 1 and 2 follow the branch the owner's two readings select, and under the CLI reading with no new command name there is nothing to build in them.

```
 Which event counts as "first use"?       Mechanism that fires there              User-level artifact needed?
 ---------------------------------------  --------------------------------------  ---------------------------
 user types the documented command        A  manual `bmad install ...`            no
 (1) a `bmad` CLI command is run          Stage 0 only; the existing manual       no
                                          `bmad install` is the only mechanism
                                          found (inference: owner decides if it
                                          counts); C only for a new, separately
                                          named command
 (2) a named bootstrap skill is invoked   B  stub skill (HOME), or I (image)      yes for B; no for I (doc-only)
     (its own name; not a `/bmad-*` name)
 (3) a session is opened in a project     D  hook (HOME or image), or G text      yes for D1, D3, G; no for D2
```

| Stage | Action | Why (comparison rows) |
|---|---|---|
| 0, now, under every reading | (a) Record the decision as OD-4 with a memlog bullet and a re-derived SPEC (common fact 12) worded "no global or user-level install of BMad framework content; first-use creation per project", and answer the "available globally" text of #478 as declined. (b) Do Option A's documentation half: reconcile `README.md:241`, keep the manual command in the README and the welcome text, fix the "already set up there" wording, which ignores the install guards and a failed install (02 C7). (c) Define once the install command and FIRST_USE(P) of common facts 3 and 4 (recorded, under the CLI reading with no shipped block, as the CAP-8 success text, which states that they are plan defaults until Section 9 rows 5, 6 and 10 are answered, plus `(assumption)` and `(question)` memlog bullets for the open defaults and one `(constraint)` bullet for the code-pinned prohibitions of step 3.6 (the open, doc-only `bmad-` prefix rule of step 3.6 is an `(assumption)` bullet, not a constraint), in the gap-closure spec, Section 8 Phase 3), with plan defaults, not settled decisions, for D-1 (atomicity: no atomic step, the failure ceiling of `install.sh:41` is inherited), D-2 (guard: bare `_bmad/` existence for FIRST_USE; `_bmad/config.toml` and the manifest are checked only to report a broken install), D-3 (subfolder of a repo: the repo top) and D-10 (hidden or visible files: hidden). All four stay open owner questions (Section 9 rows 10, 6 and 5), so Stage 0 states the defaults and the owner can still change them. | Every other option still needs the documents because a mechanism may not fire (05 b3); the command and predicate are identical for B, C, D, G and I (03 A3). |
| 1, after the owner answers | Build only the trigger the owner's two readings select (table "Dependence on the owner readings"). Evidence-ranked choice inside each family: skill trigger **B3** before B2 before B1; CLI trigger: none found to build; the existing manual `bmad install` is the only mechanism [synthesis inference: the owner decides whether it meets the decision] (Stage 0 is the whole recommendation for that reading unless the owner says otherwise), and **C** (the wrapper at `Dockerfile.coder:142`; the helper variant that step 4C.3 plans is rated separately in Table 1) only if the owner asks for a new, separately named command; session trigger **D3**, or D2 if the owner treats the managed level as not "user level" and accepts one layer. | Effort (rated L as planned for B3 and D3, because each needs a template push and a catalogue-hash retag, and for D2 (8 files), and M for C with a new command; Effort therefore does not favour B3 or D3 over B1 and D1, which are S; the Risk and Pinned-tests rows are what separate them), Risk, Pinned tests, Writes-inside-snapshot, Image rebuild and Image layers rows. |
| 2, inside the plan | Only if a Phase 4 branch is built (under the CLI reading with no new command, none): add the missing delivery proof for the chosen option (04 A.11: pin beside `test-52:1374-1377` for B3 and D3; `test-startup-boot.sh:2775` and `test-22:271` for B2; `test-73` second-file check for D2; replacement of `test-57:450` only if a boot-time write is chosen). Before relying on the chosen branch, observe on its candidate build (drafted, not shipped) the Claude Code facts that only docs answer today, by the Phase 4 gate procedure of Section 8 (a CI job modelled on `coder-ci-orca-proof.yml`, or an operator's interactive session with a saved transcript where `claude -p` cannot show the fact; no one-off scripts): that the image's Claude Code loads the stub or runs the hook, whether new project skills appear in the same session, and whether Claude follows the stub text (gaps G-01 to G-04, G-08, UV-17). Observe the run-only facts listed under the comparison. | Common facts 7, 11; the doc-only caveats of Table 1. |

Not recommended: **E** (installs framework content under HOME and leaves the project's `_bmad/` absent), **F** (no code path in 6.9.0, 6.10.0 or 6.12.4), **B1** and **D1** (certain break of `test-57:450`, the only "writes nothing" oracle, and a rewrite of HOME at every boot), **B2** (6 files, copy-if-absent text frozen for the life of a workspace, image-wide section 6 that also runs for `minimal` (R4) and a new layer as written (R5); High even if the unverified fold holds, because R2 and R4 still apply), **G** (two existing equality checks, instruction text with no enforcement), **H** (user-wide BMAD skills, version 6.8.0 versus pin 6.9.0, does not create `_bmad/`), **I** (rests on a docs table; unevaluated by the codebase research).

### Rationale

| # | Reasoning | Comparison rows it rests on |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The decision asks for creation on first use. For reading (1), the CLI reading, the only mechanism found is the existing manual `bmad install` [synthesis inference: whether running it by hand meets the 2026-11-07 decision is the owner's call, Section 9 row 2]; Option A fails the decision for the slash-skill and session-open readings, which need a built trigger. Stage 0 adds records and a specification, not run-time behaviour. A stays as the documentation half of Stage 0. | Effort/Risk row A; common fact 12 |
| 2 | E and F fail on code: F has no consumer of `global_target_dir` anywhere (01 section 2; web-02 tarball Grep for 6.9.0, 6.10.0, 6.12.4); E writes framework content under HOME and does not create the project `_bmad/` the skills read. | "Meets no global install" row |
| 3 | B1 and D1 are the only in-repo mechanisms with a certain pinned-test break (`test-57:450`), because they are delivered by `catalogue/bmad/install.sh`, the one script `test-57` runs; B2, B3, D2, D3 never pass through it. | Pinned tests row; Writes-inside-snapshot row |
| 4 | Among skill deliveries B3 has the lowest risk (Medium, R2 only: no certain break, 0 layers), is refreshed at each agent start and is count-gated by profile. Its price is a split of BMad logic across `modules.tf` and the slug, the absence of any existing delivery proof, and the highest Effort of the skill deliveries as planned (L, against S for B1): `modules.tf` is a template push, but the welcome-text edit of step 4B.6 changes `catalogue_sha256` (`main.tf:74`) and retags the image. | Risk, Image layers, HOME persistence rows (the grounds); Effort, Image rebuild rows (the price) |
| 5 | The hook family acts in every project, so it installs everywhere instead of on first use, runs without a user prompt (documented by inference only), and for D1 and D3 shares `settings.json` with writers that have no lock; D2 costs one of two free layers, is image-wide, and depends on an unconfirmed doc claim that a drop-in may hold hooks. That is why D is only recommended for reading (3), and then in the D3 or D2 shape. | Risk row; Which-reading row; Survives row |
| 6 | C is the only Claude-independent mechanism with no HOME artifact and no certain break, but it serves the narrowest reading, and under that reading the only mechanism found is the existing manual `bmad install` [synthesis inference: the owner decides whether it meets the decision]: `bmad install`, `--version` and `--help` must pass through unchanged, so C adds something only for a new, separately named command (interception of `status` or `uninstall` is not used, see Option C step 2). | Pinned tests row; Which-reading row |
| 7 | Nothing in the repo or in the superclaude package needs to change for B, C or D, and a user skill or hook under a new name survives `superclaude install --force`. | Needs-change-in-IronClaude-or-IronOrca row; Survives row |
| 8 | Everything beyond A and C leans on Claude Code behaviour that only official docs answer. Those facts are used here as caveats and as items for live observation, not as grounds for a rating. | Tag key in 6.0; doc-only rows |

Prior positions overridden by name (research-notes F9-3), so the spec for #478 can cite them: R1 and R2 (behaviour "documented", first-use creation "open owner decision"); R4 (`SPEC.md:77` for the first-use part, not the seed install); R5 (`spec-orca-catalogue-bundle/SPEC.md:141` for repos cloned after boot, not for Orca-created worktrees); R6 ("Restrictions overridden: None", "no BMAD change"); R8; R9 (README-only scope); R10 ("MUST NOT change" for `install.sh` and `manifest.yaml`); R12 (the "DANGEROUS" label for a user-level skill; it is presented here on its merits). Not overridden: R3 (global and user-level install of BMAD framework content stays declined; whether a content-free stub is "user level" is open), R7 (the boot seed install stays), R15 (report truthfully; `install.sh:60` prints "install ok" even with no seed), R16 (skills need the project's own `_bmad/`).

### Feasibility statement

**Can the ask be met without a global BMAD install?** Yes. First-use creation is per project: the image-baked CLI runs `bmad install --directory <project> --modules bmm --tools claude-code --yes`, which by the code writes only inside that directory. Evidence: 01 line 386 (Summary): "a non-interactive install is `bmad install --directory <dir> --modules bmm --tools claude-code --yes`, and by the code it writes only inside that directory (`_bmad/`, `_bmad-output/{planning,implementation}-artifacts/`, `docs/`, `.claude/skills/bmad-*`), never touches git or `~/.claude`"; 01 line 79: "in 6.9.0 there is NO consumer of `global_target_dir` ... Option F ... has no code path", so a global or user-scope BMAD install is neither available nor needed (research-notes F9-4: per-project creation "is not in conflict with the 'never globally' half of OD-1"). [CODE-VERIFIED; nothing was run.]

**Can it be met without a change in IronClaude or IronOrca?** Yes. Evidence: 03 section 6, lines 178 and 179: user-level skill, "NEEDS OTHER-REPO CHANGE: **NO**. Every placement mechanism above is in this repo ... and superclaude leaves a non-packaged skill name alone"; user-level hook, "**NO**. A hook entry with a new command string is preserved by superclaude's additive merge". It would become YES only if the skill or hook were shipped from IronClaude or superclaude started pruning unknown skill directories, which it does not today (03 line 180, `install_skills.py:97`, `install_skill.py:48`). Option C and the other image-level hook points are also inside this repo (02 lines 324-325). No IronOrca file was needed for any option.

**Stop condition: not triggered.** No re-scope is needed on the evidence in hand.

Limits that keep this open (none contradicts the statement):

| # | Limit | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | A slash skill cannot be invoked in a project that never had BMAD unless something outside the project supplies its name: BMAD skills land in the project only through the install (`.claude/skills/bmad-*`, 01 section 5), and the live `~/.claude/skills` holds no `bmad-*` entry (03 section 4). So the reading "invoking a BMAD slash skill" needs a user-level or managed artifact (B or I), or a session-start install (D) that makes the skills exist earlier. What B and I can give is a bootstrap skill under its own name, not `/bmad-help` or any other literal `/bmad-*` name: in a never-BMAD project a literal `/bmad-*` name would need a user-level `bmad-*` artifact, which Phase 3 step 3.6 of the plan forbids because a personal skill outranks a same-named project skill (web-01 section 1.2, doc-only). Whether the owner accepts a bootstrap name instead of `/bmad-*`, or accepts that shadowing risk, is an owner choice the evidence cannot settle (Section 9 rows 2 and 3). | 01 section 5; 03 section 4 |
| 2 | Whether a user-level stub or hook works at all in the image depends on Claude Code behaviour that research could only read in official docs: user skills are loaded for all projects, hooks run for all projects, hooks wait for workspace trust, a new top-level `.claude/skills/` needs `/reload-skills` unless a SessionStart hook returns `reloadSkills`, hooks in a managed drop-in are documented by inference. [UNVERIFIED - doc-only, no code confirmation; the image pins Claude Code 2.1.289 at `Dockerfile.coder:110`, and whether this behaviour holds at that version is not observed.] If a live check in the image shows the stub is not loaded or the hook does not run, B and D fail and only A and C remain in-repo; then the readings (2) and (3) would need a re-scope discussion with the owner. | web-01 key findings (a) to (f) |
| 3 | The owner may combine "no user-level artifact of any kind" with "first use = invoking a named bootstrap skill" or "opening a session". Only I and D2 remain for that combination, both resting on doc-only claims, so it is not supported by code-traced evidence and the owner decides: for "invoking a named bootstrap skill" the plan builds nothing (Phase 4 marks it BLOCKED), for "opening a session" it proceeds as D2 under the doc-only caveat. | table below, rows 3 and 5 |
| 4 | Version skew and a partial `_bmad/` are not protected by the installer (common fact 11); FIRST_USE(P) is the only barrier. | 01 F5-D, F5-F |
| 5 | 6.9.0 behaviour that only a real run shows (duration, exact file list, no-`.git` case, partial-tree repair) is UNVERIFIED. | gaps section 7 |

### Dependence on the owner readings

Open owner questions, copied unanswered (gaps section 5; BUILD-REQUEST.md:14 holds the only decision text): **Q1** is a content-free user-level stub (a skill, command or hook under `~/.claude/`, with no BMAD content) allowed, given the 2026-09-25 text "never globally or at user level" (`.memlog.md:32`) and the 2026-11-07 text "Global installation of BMAD is NOT requested"? **Q2** what counts as first use: running a `bmad` CLI command, invoking a named bootstrap skill (the task file words this as "a BMAD slash skill"; see the note under Limits, row 1), or opening a session in a project? The documents themselves say "per project only, never globally" about framework content while the CLI is global (05 C1), which hints at a narrower meaning of "user level" but does not decide it.

Stage 0 adds records and a specification, not run-time behaviour; Option A fails the decision for the slash-skill and session-open readings (Rationale 1). ## 8. Implementation Plan

**Plan basis (read first).** This plan implements the recommendation in Section 7: **Stage 0 under every reading of the owner's two answers** (record the decision, reconcile the documents, define the shared first-use command and predicate once), then **build only the one trigger that the owner's two answers select**: **B3** (stub skill written by a new root `coder_script` in `modules.tf`) for a named-bootstrap-skill trigger (not a literal `/bmad-*` name, Section 9 rows 2 and 3), for a CLI trigger **nothing beyond Stage 0**, because the only mechanism found is the existing manual `bmad install` [synthesis inference: the owner decides whether it meets the decision], and **C** (the `bmad` wrapper at `Dockerfile.coder:142`, with the block in a helper per step 4C.3) only if the owner asks for a new, separately named command, **D3** (SessionStart hook merged by a root `coder_script`) for a session-open trigger. Not built, with the reasons in Section 7: E, F, B1, D1, B2, G, H, I. Names in angle brackets, such as `<stub-name>`, `<resource-name>` and `<name>`, are names the implementer fixes under the rules of step 3.6; the research files use `<name>` in the same way and do not choose one.

**Feasibility and branch.** The Section 7 Feasibility statement is "feasible without a global BMAD install and without a change in IronClaude or IronOrca; stop condition not triggered" (Section 7; R01 Summary; R03 section 6). So this section is an implementation plan and not a re-scope proposal. The one place where the evidence does not settle feasibility is the owner combination "no user-level artifact" with a named-bootstrap-skill or session trigger. Wording used for it everywhere in this report: "not supported by code-traced evidence; owner decides". Phase 4 treats the two halves differently: (no, named bootstrap skill) is BLOCKED, because no in-repo option is supported by code-traced evidence (Section 9, row 3); (no, session) is not blocked and proceeds as branch 4D with D2, which rests on doc-only claims (Section 9, rows 3 and 15), unless the owner decides otherwise.

**What is not decided and therefore not planned as fixed.** The owner has not answered Q1 (is a content-free user-level stub allowed) or Q2 (what counts as "first use"). Which phases wait for the answers: Phases 1 and 2, steps 5.1 and 5.6, and steps 6.1 to 6.6 do not depend on them. Phase 3 needs the owner inputs of Section 9 rows 5 and 6 (git rule in step 3.4; subfolder condition in step 3.2) and is a shipped block only if a Phase 4 branch is built. Steps 5.2 to 5.5, step 6.7 and the rollback steps 7.3 to 7.5 follow the branch the owner's answers select, and do not apply when none is built. Phase 4 has three alternative branches (4B, 4C, 4D); at most one starts after the answers (under the CLI reading none starts unless the owner asks for a new command name), and no branch is started before then.

### Phase 1: Owner gate and decision records

**Goal:** record the 2026-11-07 decision as superseding OD-1 for first-use creation only, the way this repo records owner decisions, and put the two owner questions to the owner before any trigger is built.
**Dependencies:** none. The wording of the records must not state that a stub is allowed or what "first use" means, because those two answers are open (Section 9, rows 1 and 2).

All paths are relative to the worktree root. Process rule behind the order: `SPEC.md` of the gap-closure spec "derives from `.memlog.md`, so do not hand-edit it" (`SPEC.md:12`), so the memlog is written first (R05 c1).

### Phase 2: User-facing documentation (the documentation half, valid under every reading)

**Goal:** make the user-facing text true today and independent of the trigger: BMad framework content is created per project, at boot for the seed repo only, and in any other project by the one manual command. The sentence about first-use creation is added in Phase 4, in the same change as the mechanism.
**Dependencies:** Phase 1 wording. These edits can ship before Phase 4.

| Step | Action | Files | Details |

It lists BMad and says nothing about where or when BMad is configured. Keep exactly five cells with leading and trailing pipes (header at line 237 has five columns), no raw pipe and no line break inside a cell, and the blank line after the table (line 242). The cell gets a short pointer such as "BMad framework files are per project: see the note below the table". A long explanation does not go into the cell. [R05 (e)] |
| 2.2 | Add the note below the table | `templates/VSCode-AIDev02/README.md` (new short paragraph directly after the blank line that follows the table) | Must state: the Default profile ships the `bmad` CLI (image-baked, 6.9.0); the project files (`_bmad/`, `.claude/skills/bmad-*`, `_bmad-output/`) are not installed globally; boot sets them up in the seed repo only (hidden from git); in any other project they are created by the manual command, named literally: `bmad install --yes --directory . --modules bmm --tools claude-code --output-folder _bmad-output` run from the project root. The note must also carry the condition that the command itself does not check: "run this only in a project that has no `_bmad/` and no `.claude/skills/bmad-*`; if `_bmad/` already exists, the same command updates it with the image's 6.9.0 installer, which has no installed-versus-installer version test" (mechanism: R01 F5-D, F5-F; `ui.js:233-262`, [CODE-VERIFIED] by reading, never run here). This is wording, not a technical guard: the documented manual command stays unguarded (F-04 in Section 4), and whether to ship a guarded form of it is not researched. It must not say BMad is available "everywhere" or "in every project already". The command must be in the README itself because the build context is `templates/VSCode-AIDev02/image` (`main.tf:119`) and the README sits one level above it, so a workspace user can read the baked welcome text but not this README (inside-workspace invisibility of non-copied files is [UNVERIFIED], R05 U15). ### Phase 3: Shared first-use contract (one command, one predicate, one git rule)

**Goal:** define once what every trigger runs, so the branches of Phase 4 differ only in how they are delivered and fired (Section 7, Stage 0 item (c); the command and predicate are identical for B, C, D, G and I, R03 A3).
**Dependencies:** Phase 1. Owner inputs needed for step 3.4 (git rule) and step 3.2 condition 1 (subfolder): Section 9, rows 5 and 6. Nothing is written to the product yet; the contract below is the specification that Phase 4 embeds in one block. **Conditional on a built branch:** the block exists only if a Phase 4 branch is built (4B, 4D, or 4C for a new, separately named command). Under the CLI reading with no new command name, no Phase 4 branch is built and there is no shipped block: steps 3.1 to 3.6 then stay a documented specification (Stage 0 (c)), and the "embedded in the Phase 4 block" wording in the table has no target. **Where that specification is recorded in that case:** in the gap-closure spec, through its memlog (step 1.1): the success text of the `(capability) CAP-8` bullet carries the command of step 3.1 and the predicate FIRST_USE(P) of step 3.2, and that success text says the command and predicate are plan defaults until Section 9 rows 5, 6 and 10 are answered. The memlog is the append-only decision of record (`.claude/skills/bmad-spec/SKILL.md:54`; `_bmad/scripts/memlog.py:164-177` has no edit or delete command, and step 7.1 reverses only by superseding) and steps 3.2 to 3.4 are open owner defaults, so they are not written as constraints: each default (the repo-top rule of step 3.2, the bare-existence guard, the broken-install rule and failure ceiling of step 3.3, the git rule of step 3.4) is appended with `memlog.py append --type assumption` and the text "plan default pending owner, Section 9 rows 5, 6, 10"; each owner input (rows 5, 6 and 10) with `--type question`; only the code-pinned prohibitions of step 3.6 (seed-repo-path, `~/.claude` scope, test-3 reserved words, packaged and Orca names, `sc-` names) with `--type constraint`, and the `bmad-` prefix rule of step 3.6, which is open and rests on doc-only W01-2, with `--type assumption` ("plan default pending owner, Section 9 rows 2, 3"). The owner's answer (step 1.8) is a new `--type decision` bullet that supersedes the assumption. The step 1.3 run renders all of them into `SPEC.md`. No product file is written. If a Phase 4 branch is built, the shipped block is the record of the same text and the memlog bullets point to it.

| Step | Action | Files | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3.1 | Fix the one command | embedded in the Phase 4 block (no file of its own) | `NPM_CONFIG_OFFLINE=true timeout 120 bmad install --yes --directory "$P" --modules bmm --tools claude-code --output-folder _bmad-output --user-name "$(git -C "$P" config user.name \|\| id -un)" </dev/null`. This is the flag set of `catalogue/bmad/install.sh:42` (flags exist in `bmad-method/tools/installer/commands/install.js:14-37`, [CODE-VERIFIED]; never executed in this task). Rules: keep `--output-folder _bmad-output` because `--user-name` is passed (mechanism `official-modules.js:1409`, outcome of dropping it UNVERIFIED, R-9); keep `--modules bmm` (a bare `--yes` may reach external modules over the network and a GitHub API limit of 60 calls per hour per IP, G-12, W02-9); never pass `--action` (a 6.9.0 `--yes` over an existing tree goes to quick-update with no version test, `ui.js:233-262`); no `npx`; no `--directory ~` (option E) and no user-scope flag (none exists in 6.9.0 or 6.12.4, W02-3). `timeout 120` is copied from `install.sh:42` and is an author assumption; the real duration in a workspace is UNVERIFIED (G-09, R-1). Note: `global_target_dir` and `ancestor_conflict_check` are inert upstream data (F-10, F-11) and no step relies on them. |
| 3.2 | Define FIRST_USE(P) | embedded in the Phase 4 block | True only when ALL hold (R03 A2): (1) P is the git work-tree top (`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`) when inside a repo, otherwise the current directory (a subfolder resolves to the repo top so the root-anchored excludes and `find_project_root`, which stops at `_bmad` or `.git`, agree; whether a subfolder session should instead target the subfolder is an owner decision, D-3, Section 9 row 6); (2) `[ ! -e "$P/_bmad" ]` (the same test as `install.sh:24`, first half; it also covers "`_bmad/config.toml` absent", which lives inside `_bmad/`). It stays bare existence on purpose (plan default for D-2, open in Section 9 row 10): it errs on the side of never running the installer over any existing `_bmad/`, whereas a guard on `_bmad/config.toml` would let a partial tree read as first use, and `--yes` over a partial `_bmad/` takes quick-update, whose effect on a half-written tree is UNVERIFIED (R-10, G-13); step 3.3 uses `config.toml` and the manifest only to tell a broken install from a complete one; (3) no entry `"$P"/.claude/skills/bmad-*` on disk, tracked or untracked (a filesystem glob; `install.sh:24` only counts tracked files, so after the slug's own install with `_bmad/` deleted it would read "never had BMad"); (4) P is writable; (5) P is not `$HOME` and not `/` (a plan addition, not in R03 A2: with the "otherwise the current directory" fallback of condition 1, a command or session started in HOME would otherwise create `~/_bmad`, `~/_bmad-output`, `~/docs` and `~/.claude/skills/bmad-*`, which is the home-directory install of option E that K-01 excludes, R01 s6; web-01 recommendation 4 likewise says a user-level artifact "must test for the project type"). What else counts as "a project" for a folder with no `.git` is an owner question (Section 9, row 6). It widens `install.sh:24` and drops the `.git` requirement of `install.sh:22` (a directory without `.git` ends in "nothing to configure" in the slug, so the slug's branch order cannot be reused). Whether `bmad install` itself works with no `.git` is UNVERIFIED (R-3, observed in CI by the Phase 5 test). |
| 3.3 | State the broken-install case and the failure ceiling | embedded in the Phase 4 block | `_bmad/` exists but `_bmad/_config/manifest.yaml` or `_bmad/config.toml` is absent is a BROKEN install, not first use: FIRST_USE(P) is false, nothing is rewritten, and the block prints that the install in P is incomplete and names the manual command (R03 A2; `resolve_config.py:156` exits 1 without `config.toml`, so a guard on bare `_bmad/` is too weak as a completeness test, D-2: it would call a partial tree complete, which is why this step checks `config.toml` and the manifest for the message while FIRST_USE(P) stays false for any existing `_bmad/`). No auto-repair: this inherits the ceiling written at `install.sh:41` and `slug-configuration.md:55`. There is also no atomic-install step in this plan (plan default for D-1: the installer is not atomic, F-05; whether to add one is Section 9 row 10). What quick-update does to a half-written tree is UNVERIFIED (R-10, G-13), so naming the manual command as the recovery path is a stated ceiling, not a proven repair; whether to add repair is Section 9 row 10. After a failed run the block must not report success: check after the install that `P/_bmad/_config/manifest.yaml` (as `install.sh:46` does) and `P/_bmad/config.toml` both exist (SC-02), and exit non-zero with a message otherwise; the skill count (SC-03) is asserted by the Phase 5 test, step 5.3 case (a). |
| 3.4 | Fix the git rule for P | embedded in the Phase 4 block | Variant H (hide): before the install append `/_bmad/`, `/_bmad-output/`, `/.claude/skills/bmad-*/` to the file given by `git -C "$P" rev-parse --git-path info/exclude` unless already present as an exact line, then compare `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all` before and after (the pattern of `install.sh:28-34, 38, 48-53`). Variant V (visible): write no exclude lines; the new directories show as untracked. The owner has not decided (G-26, D-10, Section 9 row 5); build Variant H until the owner says otherwise because it is the only behaviour with an existing oracle (`test-57:324`, `test-startup-boot:2089-2098`) and it is undone by deleting three lines. A directory with no `.git` has no exclude file: no git step. Whether git anchors the root-anchored patterns at the work-tree top is git documentation, not run here [UNVERIFIED, G-21]. |
| 3.5 | Keep the block self-contained and testable | the same block | One fenced shell block that (a) resolves P, (b) evaluates FIRST_USE(P), (c) applies the step 3.4 rule, (d) runs the step 3.1 command, (e) checks the step 3.3 outputs, (f) prints the installer output and ends with the instruction "run `/reload-skills` or restart Claude Code in this folder" (the installer itself says "Launch your AI agent from your project folder", `installer.js:1233`; a new top-level `.claude/skills/` is not watched by a running session, W01-3, doc-only). Keeping it in one block is a design choice of this plan so that the Phase 5 test can extract and run the exact text that ships. |
| 3.6 | Fixed prohibitions | the same block, and review checklist | Never write `~/.aidev/status/seed-repo-path` (single writer is `startup.sh:673`, R05 b3); never write under `~/.claude/` other than what the chosen branch of Phase 4 defines; the stub, wrapper or hook name must not contain the whole words `preview`, `share`, `share-port`, `expose`, `dev-preview` in a `cp`, `ln -s` destination, alias or function name (`test-3-reserved-names:27, 55-90`), must not equal one of the 39 packaged superclaude skill names or `orca-cli`, `orchestration`, `computer-use`, and per W01-2 must not start with `bmad-` (a personal skill outranks a same-named project skill, doc-only; this is why the plan gives a bootstrap name and not a literal `/bmad-*` name, and whether the owner wants a literal `/bmad-*` name despite the shadowing risk is open, Section 9 rows 2 and 3; this one rule is recorded in the memlog as `--type assumption` ("plan default pending owner, Section 9 rows 2, 3"), not as a constraint, while the other prohibitions of this step are code-pinned and recorded as `--type constraint`); a stub must not use an `sc-` name either, because superclaude removes an `sc-<x>` skill that has a matching `commands/<x>.md` and does not self-activate as "stale" (`install_skills.py:88-110`, `install_skill.py:46-52`, R03 s5). |

### Phase 4: The owner-selected trigger (at most one branch; none under the CLI reading with no new command; none starts before Section 9 rows 1 and 2 are answered; none is shipped or relied on before the gate below has observed its facts on the candidate build)

**Goal:** deliver and fire the Phase 3 block at the moment the owner calls "first use", with the evidence-ranked mechanism of that family, without a global BMAD install and without a change in IronClaude or IronOrca.
**Dependencies:** Phases 1 to 3 complete; the owner's answers to Q1 and Q2 recorded (step 1.8).

Branch selection (from the Section 7 table "Dependence on the owner readings"):

| Q1: content-free user-level stub allowed | Q2: what counts as first use | Branch | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| any | running a `bmad` CLI command | No branch (Stage 0 only); 4C only if the owner asks for a new, separately named command | No user-level artifact, so Q1 does not matter for it. The existing manual `bmad install` creates the directories when run [synthesis inference: whether that meets the decision is the owner's call] and must pass through the wrapper unchanged, so 4C adds something only for a new command name (Section 9, row 2) |
| yes | invoking a named bootstrap skill | 4B (B3) | The stub is the only way a slash name exists in a project that has no `.claude/skills/bmad-*` yet, and it is the stub's own name, not `/bmad-help` or any other `/bmad-*` name: a literal `/bmad-*` name in a never-BMAD project needs a user-level `bmad-*` artifact, which step 3.6 forbids (W01-2, doc-only). An owner who wants literal `/bmad-*` names must decide this explicitly (Section 9, rows 2 and 3) |
| no | invoking a named bootstrap skill | BLOCKED | No in-repo option is supported by code-traced evidence (I rests on a docs table; D2 changes the reading to opening a session). Do not build B. Put the combination back to the owner (Section 9, row 3) |
| yes | opening a session in a project | 4D with D3 | Only if the owner accepts "install everywhere" |
| no | opening a session in a project | 4D with D2 | Only if the owner treats the managed level as not "user level" and accepts one image layer and an image-wide hook |

**Gate before relying on any branch (observed on the candidate build of the chosen branch, steps 4B.1 and 4B.2, or 4D.1 to 4D.3 for D3, or 4D.6 for D2, drafted and not shipped):** the Claude Code facts that only official docs answer today (does the image's Claude Code load a user-level stub or run the hook; do new project skills appear in the same session; does Claude follow the stub text) are [UNVERIFIED - doc-only] (W01; GL G-01 to G-04, G-08). They must be observed, not assumed. The research files describe no mechanism for observing Claude Code behaviour in CI; `test-startup-boot.sh` runs runner-native over `coder ssh` on a really booted workspace (R04 A.4) and does not drive Claude Code. A precedent that does drive it was read while fixing this report: `.github/workflows/coder-ci-orca-proof.yml` (name "Coder Orca In-Session Proof", job `orca_proof`) boots one `orca` workspace on dind-sidecar and runs one `claude -p` call, and its driver emits `ROW ... pass` lines only for what was observed and `incomplete` otherwise, which turns the job red (file header lines 1-15). Whether `claude -p` can show the three facts below is UNVERIFIED. The observation procedure of this plan (a plan definition, not a research finding):

| Item | Definition |
|---|---|
| Who and where | The implementer of the chosen branch, in a workspace booted from the candidate template version on dind-sidecar, inside a scratch git repository that has no `_bmad/` and no `.claude/skills/bmad-*` |
| How | Preferred: extra rows in a new job modelled on `coder-ci-orca-proof.yml` job `orca_proof` (one `claude -p` call over `coder ssh`, rows `pass` or `incomplete`), run through the `.github` workflows as project `CLAUDE.md` requires. Fallback for any fact that `claude -p` cannot show (the interactive workspace-trust hold-back of hooks, W01-18, is not exercised by a non-interactive call and stays UNVERIFIED): an interactive session driven by an operator, whose saved transcript is the evidence; this fallback is a manual live session, not a one-off validation script |
| Fact 1: the stub is loaded (B) or the hook runs (D) | Pass: in the scratch repository, invoking the stub by its own name makes Claude evaluate FIRST_USE(P) (B), or opening a session makes the SessionStart hook run the Phase 3 block and `_bmad/_config/manifest.yaml` exists afterwards (D). Fail: neither is observed |
| Fact 2: new project skills appear in the same session | Pass: after the install, a `bmad-*` skill is usable in the same session without a restart (with `reloadSkills` for D, or after `/reload-skills`). Fail: only a restart shows them. A fail here is not a build blocker: steps 4B.4 and 4D.3 then use the restart wording |
| Fact 3: Claude follows the stub text (B) | Pass: Claude runs the Phase 3 command in a Bash call and reports the installer output, or the user is prompted for the call. Fail: it declines, rewrites the command or offers a reinstall |
| Evidence location | The job's ledger and log artifact (or the saved transcript for the fallback), with its path written into the decision records of Phase 1 |
| Rule | An unobserved fact is `incomplete` and counts as not passed. A failed Fact 1 discards the draft of the chosen branch (nothing of it ships). Fact 1 failing means B and D fail and only A and C remain in this repo; then readings 2 and 3 need a re-scope discussion with the owner (Section 7 Feasibility, limit 2). Fact 3 failing means B fails. D2 additionally needs its own check that a managed drop-in may hold `hooks` (W01-16, inference only) |

| 4D.1 | Add a new, separate `coder_script` that merges one hook entry | `templates/VSCode-AIDev02/modules.tf` (same placement rules as step 4B.1) | `jq` merge into `~/.claude/settings.json` modelled on `catalogue/ironclaude/install.sh:100-136`: refuse a malformed file or a non-object `hooks` before writing, keep a symlinked `settings.json` a symlink (`:68-86, 105-109`), and check after the merge that every pre-existing hook command is still present (`:110-136`). `jq` is in the image (`Dockerfile.coder:590`). Event: `SessionStart`. This resource alone is a template push; 0 layers; step 4D.7 then adds a `manifest.yaml` edit that retags the image (`main.tf:74`). [R02 6.3; R03 s3, s5] |
| 4D.2 | Keep the hook out of IronClaude's predicate | the hook entry and its script path | The command string must not contain `/.claude/hooks/`: IronClaude treats any such command as its own (`ironclaude/install.sh:15-16, 25`; `spec2-health.sh:90-91`; `test-57:140`), so a bmad hook with that substring would make the IronClaude `hook` observation true without IronClaude and weaken `test-57:187, 376`. The script must also not sit in `~/.claude/hooks/` under one of the 14 managed names (`install_hooks.py:42-87`). The path where the script lives is a design choice not settled by the research. |
| 4D.3 | Hook behaviour | the script body | Resolve P from `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` and treat `cwd` as a hint (`cwd` and `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` can differ, W01-8, doc-only); evaluate FIRST_USE(P) cheaply and exit 0 when false; when true run the Phase 3 block. To make the new skills visible in the same session, the documented field is `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"SessionStart","reloadSkills":true}}` (W01-11, doc-only; no equivalent for UserPromptSubmit, which also has a 30 s default timeout, W01-10). This step uses the field only if the Phase 4 gate confirms it at the pinned Claude Code version (2.1.289, `Dockerfile.coder:110`; the pin is above the 2.1.152 that a user-authored issue gives for `reloadSkills`, W01-12, so the open point is whether the field works at the pin, not whether the pin is new enough [UNVERIFIED]); otherwise the hook output tells the user to run `/reload-skills` or restart. Prefer SessionStart. The `timeout` must be far above the 1 to 10 s of the 29 existing hooks (`~/.claude/settings.json`, R03 A3 D.2) or the install must run in the background; real install time is UNVERIFIED (G-09). |
| 4D.4 | State the blast radius and the trust limit | commentary and the welcome text | The hook fires in every project, so it installs in every repository that lacks BMAD, without a prompt (W01-14, best reading only); an interactive session holds back all hooks, including `~/.claude/settings.json`, until workspace trust is accepted, and whether a held-back SessionStart hook runs later is not stated (W01-18, doc-only). This is "install everywhere", not "first use of BMAD" (R03 A3 D.5); the owner must accept that reading (Section 9, row 2). The case "first use in a brand-new, never-opened folder" is the one the trust hold-back affects most; a stub (4B) is not gated by hook trust (W01 recommendation 2, Section 9, row 15). |
| 4D.5 | Shared-file hazards | the same resource, plus merge fixtures in the Phase 5 test | `settings.json` has other writers and no lock (`ironclaude/install.sh:139`: Orca, Claude Code `/config`); a lost update self-heals only if the adder re-adds the entry at the next start. `superclaude install` keeps a hook with a different command string (`install_hooks.py:379-447`) and the ironclaude slug fails if a pre-existing hook command disappears (`:104-136`). Each merge that changes the file leaves a `settings.json.bak.<stamp>` backup in superclaude's case. The `test-57:386` comment "the only installer that edits settings.json" becomes stale (C-F1-3): add merge fixtures (malformed file, symlinked file, self-heal) for this writer. |
| 4D.6 | Variant D2 when Q1 is "no" | `templates/VSCode-AIDev02/image/etc/claude-code/managed-settings.d/` (NEW file `20-<name>.json`; the directory exists with `10-retention.json`), `templates/VSCode-AIDev02/image/Dockerfile.coder` (own COPY line near `:500-501`), `templates/VSCode-AIDev02/main.tf` (`image_input_files`, pattern at line 87) | The COPY line must be exactly `COPY --chown=0:0 --chmod=0644 <rel> /<rel>` and separate from the `10-retention.json` line; the RUN that creates `/etc/claude-code` and `/etc/claude-code/managed-settings.d` is not split or reworded; the file is a valid JSON object without the key `cleanupPeriodDays` (`test-73:81-114`). Costs one image layer (68 to 69 of 70). Nothing is written under HOME. Whether Claude Code accepts hooks from a drop-in is documented only by inference (W01-16) [UNVERIFIED]; it applies to every profile and the user cannot disable it. |
| 4D.7 | Words in the user-facing text | `manifest.yaml` welcome text, `README.md` note, `CHANGELOG.md` | As step 4B.6. |

### Phase 5: Tests (every pinned assertion named, plus the missing proofs)

**Goal:** keep every existing pin green, change only what the plan changes, and add the proofs that no test gives today: delivery of the chosen mechanism (common fact 7: no test executes a skill, hook or wrapper first-use action, and none proves delivery for B3 or D3) and execution of the Phase 3 block.
**Dependencies:** Phases 2 to 4 (the assertions depend on the branch). Validation runs only through the GitHub Actions workflows (project `CLAUDE.md` forbids one-off validation scripts); Phase 6 names them. **Which steps apply:** steps 5.1 and 5.6 apply under every reading (table 5.1 stays green; the `test-57:10` comment gains the OD-4 reference). Steps 5.2 to 5.5 need a built Phase 4 branch (4B, 4D, or 4C for a new, separately named command), because they pin, execute or prove what a branch ships. Under the CLI reading with no new command name no branch is built, so steps 5.2 to 5.5 and the new tests have no target and are not added.

Certain breaks under the branches of this plan: none. The only certain break in the whole option set is `test-57-catalogue-configure.sh:450` (the whole-HOME "no seed: bmad writes nothing" oracle), and it breaks only for options that write inside HOME from `catalogue/bmad/install.sh` (B1 and D1; E only if it were run from it, with its write set UNVERIFIED; F has no code path, so its "X" cells are void, G-25); no branch of this plan edits that script (R04 A.10; R05 F1.2 row S1). That is a reading of the assertion code; no test was run [UNVERIFIED, G-20].

### Phase 6: Verification through the GitHub Actions workflows

**Goal:** prove every change in CI and observe the run-only facts that no research file could observe (GL section 7, R-1 to R-15). The project `CLAUDE.md` says validation is done via the `.github` Actions and one-off validation scripts are to be avoided, so no step below is a local script.
**Dependencies:** Phases 2 to 5.

| Step | Action | Files (workflow, job or step) | Details |
|---|---|---|---|

### Phase 7: Rollback and migration

**Goal:** make each step reversible and say what happens to workspaces that already exist.
**Dependencies:** Phases 1 to 5.

| Step | Action | Files | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| 7.1 | Roll back the records by appending, never by deleting | the memlog and `owner-decisions.md` of Phase 1 | The memlog is an append-style log (line 27 stays as history after line 32 voided it); a reversal is a new superseding `(decision)` bullet and a new OD row, and `SPEC.md` is re-derived again. [R05 c1] |
| 7.2 | Roll back the documentation | `README.md`, `manifest.yaml` welcome text, `CHANGELOG.md` | Revert the commits. The `manifest.yaml` edit changes `catalogue_sha256` (`main.tf:74`), so reverting it retags the image again and needs `coder update`; the README edit does not (outside `image/`). |
| 7.3 | Roll back 4B | `templates/VSCode-AIDev02/modules.tf`, `test-52` pin | Remove the resource and the pin. The stub file stays on every HOME volume where it was written, because nothing removes a user-level skill (bmad's marker is never removed on deselection, `startup.sh:1116-1117`). To remove it, add a one-release cleanup branch that deletes only a file whose content equals the shipped stub; the conservative-delete precedent removes a link entry only when it points at the exact target (`modules.tf:101-104`; line 105 is an unconditional `rm -rf` of the shared copy and is not a precedent). |
| 7.4 | Roll back 4C | `templates/VSCode-AIDev02/image/Dockerfile.coder`, helper file and its `image_input_files` entry | Revert and rebuild; the wrapper is an image file, so a container recreate restores the previous one after `coder update`. Nothing in HOME to clean. |
| 7.5 | Roll back 4D | `modules.tf` (D3) or the drop-in, its COPY line and its `image_input_files` entry (D2) | D3: the hook entry stays in each `~/.claude/settings.json`; remove it with a cleanup that deletes exactly its command string (the precedent is `image/bin/specstory-cleanup`, which drops only the exact SpecStory command, with a marker `~/.aidev/specstory-cleanup.done`, R03 s5). D2: the layer count returns to 68. |
| 7.6 | State what rollback does not undo | commentary in the decision records | BMad directories already created in user projects are user-owned and stay. 6.9.0 has `status` and `uninstall` subcommands (`bmad-method/tools/installer/commands/`); they were not run, so their behaviour is [UNVERIFIED]. |
| 7.7 | Existing workspaces | n/a | 4B and 4D (D3) change `modules.tf`, which is outside the image hash (`main.tf:104-107`), but as planned they also edit `manifest.yaml` (steps 4B.6, 4D.7), so the new template version carries a catalogue-hash retag (`main.tf:74`) and an image rebuild by `coder update` as well. The `modules.tf` change takes effect at the next agent start after the workspace is updated to the new template version, and not on a stopped workspace. 4C and 4D (D2) are image changes: a hash change is rebuilt by `coder update` (`main.tf:77-78`). A new workspace gets an empty HOME volume and receives the stub at its first agent start (4B). Persistent homes are not migrated by image seeds (`Dockerfile.coder:543-545`), which is why B1 and B2 (copy-if-absent) were not chosen. |
| 7.8 | Existing BMAD trees and version skew | FIRST_USE(P) | Only if a Phase 4 branch is built (under the CLI reading with no new command, none; the manual command stays unguarded, F-04): a project that already has `_bmad/` or a `.claude/skills/bmad-*` entry is never touched by that branch, so a repository on a newer tree (latest published is 6.12.4, W02-1) is never run over by 6.9.0. The count of such repositories can only grow while the image stays at 6.9.0. Bumping the pin is a separate plan: it changes the skill set and layout, moves skills to `uv run`, makes `--yes` without `--directory` install into the working directory and needs its own Renovate handling and CI run (W02-6, W02-7; D-7). |
| 7.9 | The seed repo | `catalogue/bmad/install.sh` (unchanged) | The boot seed install keeps its behaviour. If a Phase 4 branch is built (under the CLI reading with no new command, none): a seed repo that already received BMAD at boot is "has BMad" for FIRST_USE(P), so first use never reinstalls it. Assumption D-9 stands until the owner answers (Section 9, row 8). |
| 7.10 | A failed first run | Phase 3, step 3.3 | A partial `_bmad/` stays; if a Phase 4 branch is built (under the CLI reading with no new command, none), the block reports the install as incomplete and names the manual command. There is no auto-repair in this plan (ceiling inherited from `install.sh:41`). |

### Persistence and snapshot checks

This step covers the recommended option as Section 7 defines it: Stage 0 (documents only, writes nothing at run time) plus the one owner-selected trigger. Rows are given for each trigger branch because the owner has not chosen. (v)(1), (v)(2), (v)(3) are the three checks of this section: writes inside the whole-HOME snapshot, persistence, and overwrite by `superclaude install` or this repo's boot code.

**(v)(1) Paths written inside the whole-HOME snapshot that `test-57-catalogue-configure.sh:448-450` compares.** The snapshot is `snap "${H}"` (`test-57:148-153`): every path under the fixture HOME except `*/.git/index` and `./.aidev/installed/*`, with a sha256 of every regular file, plus the content of `./.aidev/installed/*.configured`; `mk_home` puts `.claude/CLAUDE.md`, a two-hook `.claude/settings.json` and the seed repo `${H}/seed` inside it (`test-57:126-136`). `test-57` runs only `/catalogue/<slug>/install.sh` through `run_slug` (`test-57:75-79`).

| Branch | Paths it writes inside that snapshot | New or changed test-57 assertion |
|---|---|---|
| Stage 0 (Phases 1 to 3) | None at run time (documents and one specified block) | None. `test-57:450` stays: `ck "no seed: bmad writes nothing" test "$(snap "${H}")" = "${want}"` |
| 4B (B3) | `~/.claude/skills/<stub-name>/SKILL.md`, written by a `coder_script` that `test-57` never runs; at first use, `P/_bmad`, `P/_bmad-output/...`, `P/docs`, `P/.claude/skills/bmad-*` (inside HOME only when P is under HOME; `/config/workspace` is a separate volume mounted outside HOME, `main.tf:178-180, 599-602`) | `test-57:450` unchanged. The new proofs are in the Phase 5 test: "first use writes only under P" (`snap` restricted to paths outside P is identical before and after) and the static pin of step 5.2 that the resource writes only the stub path |
| 4C (C in place) | None at boot. At run time, the project directories only. `/usr/local/bin/bmad` and the helper are outside HOME | `test-57:450` unchanged; the seeded runs `test-57:175, 323-324, 457-459` now pass through the wrapper and must stay green |

## 9. Open Questions

Nothing in this section is answered by the author. Rows 1 and 2 are the two owner questions copied unanswered from the task file ("Open Questions Carried Into The Research Report", lines 163 and 164) and from `gaps-and-questions.md` section 5; each lists the options and mechanism that each reading implies. Row 4 restates the Section 7 feasibility statement. Rows 15 to 19 and the register in 9.1 carry every UNVERIFIED item and every unclosed gap from the gaps log. Tags: [UNVERIFIED] = not observed; [UNVERIFIED - doc-only] = stated only in external documentation (W01, W02), never the sole basis of a plan step.

| # | Question | Impact | Suggested Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Owner Q1 (unanswered): is a user-level bootstrap artifact allowed?** The 2026-09-25 owner text says BMad is never installed "globally or at user level" (`.memlog.md:32`); the 2026-11-07 decision says "Global installation of BMAD is NOT requested" (`BUILD-REQUEST.md:14`, the only copy). Does a tiny stub skill, command or hook under `~/.claude/` that contains no BMAD content and only starts the per-project install count as "user level"? The documents say "per project only, never globally" about framework content while the `bmad` CLI itself is global (`Dockerfile.coder:139`, R05 C1), which hints at a narrower meaning but does not decide it. | Decides whether 4B and 4D (D3) can be built at all. **If yes:** options B (B3 first: lowest risk, 0 layers, no certain break; `modules.tf` is a template push and the planned welcome-text edit adds a catalogue-hash retag; B2: 6 files, text frozen for the life of a workspace; B1: certain break of `test-57:450`), D1/D3 (a hook entry in `~/.claude/settings.json`), G (instruction text in `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`). **If no:** for the CLI reading, Stage 0 only (no HOME artifact; C only for a new, separately named command); I (image-level managed skill location; the docs give the path `/etc/claude-code/.claude/skills/<skill-name>/`, doc-only and not observed in the image) and D2 (managed-settings drop-in, +1 layer, hooks accepted only by inference) for the other readings; otherwise only the documentation half (A). Options E (`bmad install --directory ~`), its variant E2 (an install in a workspace parent folder, upstream's answer to issue #1852, W02-15), F (no code path through 6.12.4) and H (upstream plugin route: user-wide BMAD skills of version 6.8.0, does not create `_bmad/`) stay out under either answer; whether E2 works for repositories with their own `.git` is UNVERIFIED (G-16). | Ask the owner.
